#5239. Solana Co-Founder Names Biggest Threat to Crypto

Solana co-founder Anatoly Yakovenko said phishing and social engineering attacks are the biggest threat to crypto and advised investors to worry more about social engineering attacks than about AI or quantum computers breaking crypto security.

"Phishing and social engineering is the biggest threat to crypto. Not AI breaking math or quantum computers," he wrote on X.

The Solana co-founder also suggested having a separate iOS device with Apple's Lockdown Mode turned on and using a multisignature crypto wallet.

This is one of the cheapest ways for investors to safeguard their digital assets, according to Yakovenko.

The Solana co-founder's statement highlights a fundamental weakness in crypto security: hackers don't need to break blockchain security to steal digital assets.

What they can do is fool crypto holders into giving them access to their assets.

Phishing involves using malicious emails, sites or messages to trick people into revealing sensitive information such as crypto wallet recovery phrases, account information, etc.

Social engineering is a broader term that describes attacks in which hackers manipulate victims into doing something that can compromise their security.

Yakovenko's point becomes crystal clear with the recent massive hack at Binance.

The world's largest crypto exchange, Binance, faced a security breach on Oct. 29 that affected around $6.34 billion in digital assets.

According to Binance's Nov. 3 investigation update, the hackers carried out a sophisticated social engineering attack, which included fake job interviews to compromise Binance employees.

A Binance software engineer was targeted by people pretending to be representatives of a fake company. The Binance employee went through some fake job interviews. The employee was convinced to download what seemed to be a legitimate technical assignment.

But the downloaded file contained malicious code that helped the hackers compromise the employee's computer.

Then, the hackers used the compromised devices, stolen credentials and an internal AI tool to access Binance's wallet infrastructure.

Eventually, this helped the hackers execute unauthorized crypto transfers from the exchange's hot wallets.

The Security Alliance (SEAL) released a report on Nov. 4 stating that its security team handled 99 incidents from Oct. 28 to Nov. 3.

In three of those incidents involving fraudulent customer support calls, victims lost roughly $1.57 million and 1.12 BTC from Oct. 28 to Nov. 3.

One of the scams involved fake Apple support representatives stealing a victim's Apple ID credentials. The victim had saved their crypto recovery phrase in cloud storage. Another victim shared their wallet recovery phrase during a fake Telegram support call.

The group also tracked 5,137 phishing domains during this reporting period.

#5238. Can You Put Crypto Mining in Space? Elon Musk's Data Center Idea Can Be Used in Crypto

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Elon Musk did what he does best on X: fresh speculations via post on November 4, saying: “Data centers in space look pretty appealing right now.”

Musk was talking about data centers, but the idea can be used in crypto too: could Bitcoin mining rigs work in orbit, powered only by the Sun? Technically, they should, why not? Economically, however, it's getting a bit more complicated.

Space has one big advantage: plenty of solar energy. Unlike mining farms on Earth, orbital mining stations could use large solar ray to make their own electricity without any power grid. Satellites today could get constant powering from sunlight, leading to a lower battery consumption.

Data centers in space looking pretty appealing right now

In theory, Bitcoin ASIC miners could then run around the clock with very low or non-existent ongoing electricity costs. The problem is that solar panels, batteries, power electronics and would all have to be launched into orbit–that's the part where problems arise.

Space might look like the perfect place to cool mining equipment, since the cosmic background is extremely cold. Unfortunately, it's not so simple and the vacuum in space actually makes cooling harder than on earth.

Here, mining rigs get rid of heat with fans, air conditioning or liquid cooling, in orbit, there's no air at all that will be able to push the air away. So the only option out there seems to be the liquid cooling, large radiator panels which should be able to release heat as infrared radiation.

According to Boston Consulting Group (BCG), a 100-kilowatt orbital data center could need around a whopping 400 square meters of radiators. But apart from technical challenges, we should always keep in mind the profitability. Getting equipment to space in the first place is what makes the whole idea questionable.

According to BCG, launching to orbit currently costs around $1,500 per kilogram. That means, a one-ton mining setup would cost around $1.5 million just to launch, excluding hardware, cooling, solar arrays and maintenance that will cost just as much if not more.

You also can't ignore space radiation, equipment failures and limited chances for repairs that add even more costs at the top of it. The only thing that can somehow make math look better is more efficient ASIC chips and ways of delivering goods to space at the fraction of today's costs.

Thankfully, Bitcoin miners need little to no bandwidth, so the connection issue is somewhat solved on its own.

Physically, mining Bitcoin in space is possible: is it justifiable financially though? Not really. Reusable rockets, lightweight cooling and other things might make it more financially viable, but as of today, we should be sticking with good old mining rigs in huge hangars on Earth.

#5237. Morgan Stanley and Chainlink advance tokenization – But will demand follow?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The trend of moving towards tokenizing traditional markets is accelerating as large financial organizations begin to implement blockchain-based systems.

Morgan Stanley has begun its transition to implementing such technology, joining forces with DTCC by utilizing its new tokenization service.

In fact, DTCC launched its DTC Tokenization Service (DTS) into live production on the 28th of October. The service utilizes blockchain technology to convert securities held by DTCC clients into custody into tokens.

If successful, it could increase the liquidity of equities and facilitate better usage of collateral in lending transactions.

In addition, Morgan Stanley’s involvement represents a significant step forward from experimental projects into practical institutional adoption. The next challenge will be to integrate these tokenized assets with existing market infrastructure.

Commenting on the move, Amy Oldenburg, the bank’s Head of Digital Assets Strategy, said the hands-on integration experience will inform the firm’s digital asset development as markets evolve.

This highlights how practical implementation could shape wider institutional adoption.

That institutional push is also reaching blockchain data providers. This is as Chainlink [LINK] expands access to Asian stock markets.

Its APAC Equities Streams now cover over $40 trillion in equity markets across China, Japan, South Korea, Taiwan, and Hong Kong. The feeds provide live prices for companies including TSMC, Tencent, Samsung, SK Hynix, and BYD.

This gives blockchain platforms access to traditional stock market data. The feeds are already available on Lighter, Jupiter Exchange, and Predict, supporting trading and prediction markets linked to traditional stocks.

Together, these developments show how banks and crypto firms are bringing financial assets onto blockchain networks, although wider adoption will depend on demand.

As institutional participation and on-chain access expand, actual market activity will determine whether tokenization gains lasting traction. To achieve this, rising trading volume must lead to consistent settlement and increasing transaction counts.

If not for sustained settlement activity, then creating more options in terms of asset availability does not necessarily result in increased demand.

Therefore, distinguishing between institutional adoption and market adoption is important when assessing adoption metrics. Morgan Stanley and DTCC are working to make traditional assets accessible to blockchain-type networks.

However, simply making an asset accessible via blockchain does not automatically create a sustainable demand for that product. Similarly, Chainlink is providing data necessary for on-chain trades.

However, infrastructure is only one component needed to create active markets. For instance, repeated trading activity by investors will support the creation of liquid markets. Additionally, higher levels of liquidity will reduce friction associated with completing trades.

Ultimately, tokenization’s success will depend less on how many assets reach blockchain networks and more on how often people use them.

Muriuki Lazaro is a on-chain data analyst with a B.Sc. in Data Science. Muriuki specializes in dissecting complex on-chain data into clear and accurate insights for readers in the crypto ecosystem, with a particular focus on Bitcoin.

#5236. Justin Sun demands court sanctions over Trump-linked World Liberty Financial’s hidden UAE owners

Justin Sun alleges World Liberty withheld foreign ownership details before using its UAE citizenship to challenge the jurisdiction of an American federal court.

Justin Sun accused the President Donald Trump-backed crypto firm World Liberty Financial of concealing UAE citizenship to escape his federal lawsuit.

The Tron founder said on Nov. 3 that his lawyers had requested sanctions against World Liberty Financial and its attorneys after the company disclosed foreign ownership connections months into litigation over his $45 million investment.

The disclosure introduced another controversy surrounding the president's cryptocurrency business, which has promoted itself as an American financial venture despite a reported $500 million investment from an Abu Dhabi-backed entity tied to a senior UAE government official.

Sun accused World Liberty of selectively invoking its national identity depending on whether it was raising money or defending itself in court.

“World Liberty was proudly American when it was selling tokens. It discovered its UAE roots only when it needed a way out of an American federal courtroom.”

The accusation followed an August court filing in which World Liberty expanded its ownership disclosure from one entity to 22, identifying citizenship in Florida, Delaware, Puerto Rico and the UAE.

Sun alleged the company initially disclosed only Florida citizenship, despite rules requiring it to identify the members of its ownership structure.

The revised filing came six days after an Aug. 20 hearing over World Liberty's attempt to move the investor dispute into arbitration. Sun said the judge had indicated that certain claims should remain in open court, while others could proceed through arbitration.

World Liberty subsequently argued that its UAE citizenship deprived the federal court of jurisdiction because foreign parties were present on both sides of the lawsuit.

Sun, who sued the company in April for allegedly inducing his investment through fraud and unlawfully freezing his tokens, said his lawyers had already spent hundreds of thousands of dollars contesting World Liberty's arbitration request.

He accused the company of failing to disclose ownership information that could have determined the court's jurisdiction at the beginning of the case.

The ownership dispute carries broader political significance because World Liberty's ties to the UAE were already attracting scrutiny over potential conflicts involving the president's family.

In January 2025, days before Trump's inauguration, an investment vehicle backed by UAE National Security Adviser Sheik Tahnoon bin Zayed Al Nahyan agreed to acquire a 49% stake in World Liberty for $500 million.

The agreement, signed by Eric Trump, directed approximately $187 million to Trump family-controlled entities, according to reporting on the transaction.

Sheikh Tahnoon, a brother of the UAE president, also oversees an investment empire with interests in artificial intelligence and advanced technology.

The transaction subsequently drew scrutiny because the Trump administration expanded the UAE's access to advanced American AI chips. Democratic lawmakers raised questions about potential conflicts between the president's business interests and US foreign policy.

World Liberty has rejected suggestions that its investment arrangement influenced US government decisions. President Trump has also said he was unaware of the transaction and that his sons manage the business.

The company's public messaging has nevertheless emphasized its American identity.

Its Gold Paper declares that World Liberty is not organized offshore and describes it as an American corporation seeking to bring decentralized finance to the US.

Those statements are not necessarily inconsistent with foreign ownership. A US-organized company can have foreign investors and acquire foreign citizenship for purposes of federal diversity-jurisdiction rules.

The reported UAE investment also came after Sun's initial November 2024 token purchase, and it became public months before World Liberty amended its court disclosure.

The unanswered question in Sun's sanctions request concerns why the company did not identify the relevant foreign ownership connections when the federal litigation began.

Sun contends that World Liberty either failed to provide its attorneys with accurate information or that its lawyers failed to adequately investigate the company's ownership structure.

World Liberty has previously rejected Sun's fraud allegations and accused him of misconduct in a separate defamation lawsuit.

Sun said he would pursue the original dispute in California state court after the federal judge addresses his sanctions request, leaving the court to determine whether the delayed ownership disclosure warrants penalties against the company or its legal team.

#5235. Hoskinson’s Midnight Tease Raises a Hard Question for ADA Holders

Home » Trendsetter News » Hoskinson’s Midnight Tease Raises a Hard Question for ADA Holders

Charles Hoskinson’s undisclosed “magnum opus” could boost Midnight’s NIGHT token before benefiting ADA.

Charles Hoskinson’s next big reveal could benefit Midnight’s NIGHT token before it helps Cardano. That is the central argument from a crypto commentator examining the Cardano founder’s claim that he is developing the “magnum opus” of his career & whether its economic rewards will reach Cardano investors.

The project remains undisclosed in the video. Hoskinson reportedly says only Midnight can deliver it, leaving the host to assess several possibilities rather than a confirmed product announcement.

Each points toward the same investment question: where does activity create token demand? Midnight’s transaction resource, DUST, is generated by holding NIGHT, giving that token a closer connection to network usage than ADA has.

Fire Hustle cites Midnight network data showing daily transactions rising from fewer than 300 to more than 26,000 in roughly a week after permissionless application deployment opened. It reports more than 47,000 daily transactions by late October, although transaction counts alone do not establish sustained demand or economic value.

For ADA holders, the more direct potential benefit is a proposed rewards arrangement involving Cardano stake pools and their delegators. According to the host, opening Midnight participation to those operators is planned for early 2027; the rewards are not presented as already operating.

Fire Hustle puts ADA near $0.20 at recording, roughly 90% below its 2021 peak. The video also cites less than $100 million locked in Cardano applications and under $2,000 in network fees on one recent day – figures that underscore the gap between anticipation and current usage.

There is precedent for expectations outrunning delivery. ADA reached its 2021 high roughly 10 days before Cardano’s smart-contract upgrade, the host notes. That history is a warning about event-driven speculation, not proof that another sell-off will follow.

The distinction for ADA investors is between a compelling reveal and measurable value reaching their holdings. NIGHT may have the nearer-term exposure to Midnight adoption; ADA’s case depends more on whether staking rewards materialize and Cardano’s own application activity grows alongside it.

#5234. Proton Users Revolt After CEO Calls Security Caution 'Loser Thinking' Amid AI Push

Proton Users Revolt After CEO Calls Security Caution 'Loser Thinking' Amid AI Push

Proton users are reporting subscription cancellations after revelations that CEO Andy Yen pressured engineers to embrace AI and dismissed a request for more security testing as “loser thinking.”

The backlash cuts into Proton’s main selling point—trusting it with information people want kept private. Its encrypted email service scrambles messages to protect their contents, and customers questioning its development practices are asking whether faster releases could come at their expense.

A Proton employee asked the company in August for a few days to pentest new code, meaning specialists would try to break into it before release, per 404 Media. Yen replied: “That’s bullshit and loser thinking. Of course it’s possible to be fast and secure.”

He also urged staff to stop writing code by hand and use more AI, and mentioned that he’d be reinterviewing its staff to see if and how they are using AI to do their job, the outlet reported. Its investigation drew on nine current and former employees, internal messages, and meeting recordings.

This didn’t land well among users, and customers have responded with cancellation notices and demands for new leadership on social media such as Reddit. A proposal on Proton’s feedback forum calling for Yen to step down had 2,020 votes when reviewed, alongside comments from users saying they had canceled subscriptions or would not renew.

Besides the blind use of AI, some former and current Proton employees on Reddit and social media have documented a toxic work culture, especially from the CEO.

Those posts document anger, rather than a verified count of departing customers. Canceling a paid plan also does not necessarily mean deleting an account.

Proton’s AI ambitions predate this dispute. Its Lumo chatbot comes with protections against using conversations to train AI, but internal use of AI was not among Proton’s selling points, even in the peak of the chatbot hype.

To address concerns over irresponsible use of AI, on November 1, technology chief Bart Butler published an engineering manifesto arguing that developers should direct large language models, AI systems that generate text and code, rather than treat them as occasional assistants.

Butler compared manual coding to memorizing phone numbers, and envisioned eventually ending most human code review. He also insisted that expertise and testing remain essential, with engineers accountable for work that models cannot reliably handle.

“Your job has fundamentally changed. It is no longer to write code, and LLMs are not here to help you write better code faster,” the manifesto reads. “Your job is to help the LLM write better code faster.” Doubling on its stance, Proton sees avoiding AI as an “existential threat” to the success of its very mission.

“‘Programming’ is dead. Engineering is not,” Buttler told his own team. “Your old job is gone”.

The document also calls for company-wide investment in automation, arguing that generating code faster achieves little if approvals and other development steps remain slow. Butler described that overhaul as the next phase of adoption.

AI tools can introduce security problems of their own. For example, Microsoft researchers said a now patched Claude flaw could expose secret access credentials through malicious instructions hidden in material the coding assistant reviewed.

They can also help find weaknesses: Google’s PageBreak system searches its applications for exploitable bugs and checks whether attacks actually work.

Proton acknowledged to 404 Media that management had sometimes communicated too bluntly and that employees were anxious. Its public manifesto nevertheless argues that failing to adopt AI quickly threatens the company’s ability to compete.

Yen canceled the re-interviews on October 30. The assessments would have required staff to explain their AI use, with employees fearing their answers could determine whether they kept their jobs.

#5233. Dogecoin Contributor Shares Key Safety Reminder Amid Binance Withdrawal Resumption

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Dogecoin community member Mishaboar has shared a fresh safety reminder to DOGE holders as cryptocurrency withdrawals resume on Binance following the exchange's recent security incident.

Binance began restoring withdrawals in phases on November 1, 2026, with that of Dogecoin already restored. Binance has also published a cryptographic Proof of Reserves for selected assets, including Dogecoin, based on its October 31 snapshot.

A few updates on the Binance situation since my last post. - The Hack Binance has finally published its initial technical findings, and, as we had expected, the attack was considerably more complicated than the original explanation involving a compromised devops laptop. According to Binance, the attackers targeted multiple engineers through malicious software, including an earlier attempt involving a tampered devtool installer and a fake job interview that delivered malware. They then exploited an internal AI tool and stolen deployment credentials to gain access to Binance's wallet-signing infrastructure. On Ethereum, malicious EIP-7702 authorizations allowed the attackers to continue moving funds even after Binance had suspended normal withdrawals. It's difficult to understand why the signing controls allowed these authorizations in the first place. I am curious to know how it is possible the authorizations were not detected more quickly, and why revoking them took so long on wallets holding such gigantic amounts. Binance says it took almost four hours to fully contain the attack. It will be good to see independent reviews from competent security analysts. - Withdrawals/Deposits (after withdrawals reopened) According to figures shared by Binance co-CEO Richard Teng and reported by CoinDesk, approximately $10.97 billion was withdrawn during the first 46 hours after withdrawals reopened, against $5.69 billion in deposits. That leaves approximately $5.28 billion in net outflows, as of early November 3. Withdrawal activity was particularly intense on Sunday, but appears to have slowed down since. Binance claims that hourly deposits had started exceeding withdrawals by the end of that period. - The Money Binance reported approximately $2.85 billion frozen or intercepted in its November 3 update. Once again, remember that frozen funds are NOT necessarily recovered funds. Binance also says it has replaced the vast majority of affected assets using its own resources, including SAFU and insurance funds (see my previous update for considerations about this). The published replenishment percentages have been somewhat inconsistent, however. And once again, replacing stolen assets is NOT the same as recovering them from the attackers. Using SAFU and the Futures Insurance Fund to cover these losses also means that the risk profile of holding funds on the exchange may have shifted considerably. As exchanges operate largely within black boxes, it is difficult to assess this at this point. - Withdrawals and Proof of Reserves Binance has published a cryptographic Proof of Reserves for ten selected assets (including Dogecoin), based on its October 31 snapshot. That is considerably fewer than the 55 assets covered in its previous snapshot, with many smaller assets still missing. Once again, this is useful, but as I have explained multiple times, Proof of Reserves are never a comprehensive audit of an exchange's financial condition and stability. More cryptocurrency withdrawals are being restored in phases, although availability still depends on the specific asset and network. Native Dogecoin withdrawals were included in the first reopening phase. The usual reminder: please make sure you select the actual Dogecoin network when withdrawing to your own wallet, rather than stuff like Binance-pegged DOGE on BSC. My recommendation stays the same: withdraw when and if you can, without rushing, and after verifying that you have good backups of your wallets and that the wallet you are using is safe.

As native Dogecoin withdrawals have now been restored, Mishaboar shared his "usual reminder" to the Dogecoin community, telling them to ensure they select the actual Dogecoin network when withdrawing to their wallets, "rather than stuff like Binance-pegged DOGE on BSC," he said.

A Binance-Pegged Dogecoin, a BEP-20 token, runs on the BNB Smart Chain (BSC), and the distinction between it and the actual DOGE coin on the Dogecoin network remains essential to prevent confusion.

Mishaboar also advised users not to rush the withdrawal process, noting that wallet security and proper backup procedures should come first: "My recommendation remains the same: withdraw when and if you can, without rushing, and after verifying that you have good backups of your wallets and that the wallet you are using is secure."

Major crypto exchange Binance recently published initial findings and a response update on the October 29 security incident.

According to Binance, the attackers targeted multiple engineers through malicious software, including an earlier attempt involving a tampered devtool installer and a fake job interview that delivered malware. They then exploited an internal AI tool and stolen deployment credentials to gain access to Binance's wallet-signing infrastructure.

The incident involves assets of some $6.34 billion. About $2.85 billion worth of assets have been intercepted or frozen as of the update.

As of Oct. 31, 2026, Binance said it has restored 98% of the affected token assets and is working on purchasing outstanding, more illiquid assets.

Binance stated that the entry points identified as having been used in the attack have been closed, and it is engaging independent third-party cybersecurity experts to conduct a comprehensive review of the incident.

#5232. Liquid restores 1:1 Bitcoin backing, but wallet refills remain manual

SideSwap reports full L-BTC backing, while customer payouts still depend on its own Bitcoin wallet and manual refills.

The Liquid Network's Bitcoin reserve again fully backs L-BTC, its Bitcoin-linked token, swap provider SideSwap said Nov. 3.

Direct withdrawals from the Liquid Federation remain restricted, leaving SideSwap's customers dependent on the provider's own Bitcoin wallet for payouts.

In its Nov. 3 update, the swap provider cited 4,270.54 L-BTC in circulation against 4,270.54 BTC in the reserve managed by the Liquid Federation that morning. Those equal balances restore one Bitcoin of reserve backing for each L-BTC, according to SideSwap.

The company identified deposits of 300 BTC and 250 BTC on Nov. 2 among the transfers that brought the reserve level with circulation.

The restoration follows the Sept. 6 incident in which an Elements software flaw allowed about 4,000 unbacked L-BTC to be created and converted into Bitcoin through SideSwap's service, according to the provider.

SideSwap says its L-BTC-to-BTC service has been open since Oct. 20, and holders can send L-BTC and receive Bitcoin at a one-to-one rate, minus the provider's standard 0.1% fee. That customer conversion route remains separate from direct Federation peg-outs, the mechanism for releasing Bitcoin from the network's reserve.

Until Federation peg-outs return, SideSwap says every customer payout comes from its own Bitcoin wallet, and that it exchanges the L-BTC it receives for Bitcoin in bulk.

When the wallet runs low, orders can sit in a queue until the next refill arrives. SideSwap says it pays those orders in arrival order after replenishment and does not give a guaranteed completion time.

Liquid is fully backed. Payouts still use SideSwap’s wallet.

SideSwap reports equal reserve and circulation balances. Its customer conversion route still depends on manually replenished Bitcoin inventory.

Restored backing and immediate payout capacity are separate: a SideSwap order may wait for its next wallet refill.

Reserve figures are SideSwap’s Nov. 3 morning report. Wallet funding states are conditional illustrations, not a live service indicator. The Federation restart date and payout waiting time are unknown.

#5231. SEC gives Canton crypto regulatory relief – Why CC faces a $0.10 test

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On the 3rd of November, the SEC Division of Corporation Finance reported that it views Canton [CC] as a digital commodity. Through a no-action letter, the division said that Canton’s value derives from the Canton Network’s operations and market dynamics.

Simply put, the SEC clarified that Canton doesn’t get value from profits generated by others’ efforts. Furthermore, the division added that the coin lacks a central party.

For that reason, the division noted that,

“ The division will not recommend enforcement action to the commission”

The decision reflects the division’s position based on facts presented by Canton. However, it does mean the SEC has made conclusions on whether the coin is a security.

The SEC decision was welcomed by market players. Miles Jennings, for example, added that the SEC’s token taxonomy from March and follow-up FAQs created a clear pathway out of securities laws.

SEC’s decision provides legal clarity for institutions rushing to tokenize on the Canton Network. In fact, the network reported that institutions have been extremely active in recent months.

Firstly, DTCC launched its tokenization service with 20 participants, including JPMorgan and Fidelity for tokenized stocks.

Secondly, Uber piloted treasury cash moves using HSBC tokenized deposits and Franklin Templeton funds. Finally, Taiwan’s TDCC joined the Canton Foundation.

These institutions join an expanding base for tokenized assets. Canton Network reported that it leads the rankings for tokenized assets.

According to RWA.xyz, RWA value on Canton is more than seven times that of the next four chains combined, with a value of around $348.9 billion. Moreover, the rising value points to growing adoption and network acceptance by major institutions.

On the charts, the market responded strongly positively to the SEC’s decision. Notably, Canton bounced back from a $0.093 slip to a local high of $0.0986.

On the daily charts, the altcoin is up 5% while the trading volume climbed 121% to $31 million. Despite these gains, the market is still experiencing strong selling pressure.

On the spot, for instance, the Sell Volume exceeded 9 million over the past 24 hours compared to 8 million in volume, implyinh that more sell orders were executed.

The same was observed on the perps side, with Sell Volume rising to 8.1 million on Binance.

The high volume indicated that investors have yet to flip with the trend and are mostly fearful of another drop, thus cashing out recent gains.

Under such circumstances, CC is unlikely to sustain the uptrend. If seller dominance persists, CC could pull back to $0.092.

However, if the trend reversal holds with investors turning optimistic over the legal clarity, CC will flip $0.1 and eye $0.11 in the short term.

#5229. South Korea orders massive crypto wallet lockdown following the Binance hack

The FSC also requested stronger transfer monitoring, response staffing and reserves, while exchange implementation and any new custody threshold remain unspecified.

South Korea's Financial Services Commission has asked domestic crypto exchanges to keep a larger share of customer assets offline and strengthen their response to suspicious activity after the Binance hack.

The FSC urged operators to increase the proportion held in cold wallets, continuously monitor deposits and withdrawals, secure additional response staff, promptly report and share abnormal access attempts, and build additional reserves.

Together, the requests address how exchanges hold customer crypto, monitor transfers, respond to suspicious access, and maintain financial protection against incidents.

The regulator's Nov. 3 clarification said it immediately requested the measures at a meeting held at 10 a.m. on Nov. 2. Participants included the Financial Supervisory Service, Financial Security Institute, a crypto industry association, and major virtual-asset businesses. The FSC dates the Binance hack that prompted the review to Oct. 30.

The November meeting also examined controls over access to computer systems, approval and signing arrangements for wallet withdrawals, and administrator privileges. Those checks extend the review beyond the share of assets held offline to the systems and permissions used to move them.

South Korea's four requests for safer crypto custody

The FSC's Nov. 2 requests, reaffirmed Nov. 3, build on a custody framework adopted in 2024.

The four requests cover custody, transfers, incident response and financial buffers.

The releases do not report exchange-by-exchange implementation. The 80% ratio is the 2024 standard, not a newly announced threshold.

Method: Source-bound qualitative safeguard map. Connectors identify the common subject of requested protections, not transfers of funds, execution order or confirmed implementation. Selectable views distinguish historical rules from current requests. No derived figures.

The FSC's July 2024 supervision-regulation notice set the custody share at 80% of customer crypto's defined economic value. The notice accompanied the legal framework taking effect on July 19, 2024.

The ratio uses a defined valuation method based on quantities and average won conversion values over the preceding year. The calculation multiplies each type's quantity by its preceding-year average won conversion value, then adds the amounts.

That gives the custody ratio a valuation basis across the different crypto assets held for customers, and also establishes standards for insurance, mutual-aid coverage, or reserves to meet liabilities arising from incidents such as hacking and computer failures.

On Oct. 28, regulators began on-site inspections after Bithumb reported signs of an employee-information leak. The Nov. 2 meeting concerns the subsequent response to Binance and the safeguards sought across domestic operators.

The tide seems to be turning for Shiba Inu (SHIB) on exchanges after a big wave of token withdrawals. Shiba Inu's on-chain data suggests that netflows are finally positive which essentially ends the biggest outflow trend that was fueled by the most recent Binance hack and closed withdrawals.

According to the latest exchange data, around 429 billion SHIB flowed into exchanges, with 276 billion being removed which corresponds to a positive netflow of roughly 153 billion tokens, eliminating the withdrawal trend present before.

Withdrawals are still heavy, though, with the outflow spike of around 220 billion tokens in the last 7 days.

Exchange reserves currently show 85 trillion, which is a normal reading for the token in the last few years. All available SHIB on exchanges at the current price is worth approximately $400 million.

Usually, outflows from exchanges are seen as a sign of accumulation among investors, since they move their tokens into wallets instead of keeping them on exchanges to sell at the best moment possible.

Rising inflows, on the other hand, are considered a negative signal, which could pinpoint an upcoming selling activity, despite the fact that deposits aren't necessarily turning into selling pressure right away.

SHIB looks bearish from a technical perspective, with the daily timeframe showing the token trading near $0.00000465, below its major moving averages. SHIB has also broken below an ascending support trendline.

The $0.0000050–$0.0000052 zone is now the key resistance, and the longer-term moving average near $0.0000055 should be considering the main target for flipping the mid to long-term trend.

The Relative Strength Index (RSI) stays in the lower part of its range. Momentum is weak, but a reversal isn't confirmed until volume turn to adequate levels.

The withdrawal wave has clearly calmed down, but not by the extent needed for a proper market reversal at this point in time.

The 153 billion SHIB netflow we are witnessing now could add even more selling pressure, so it's important to stay cautious if you're a large meme coin holder.

Unfortunately, to gain control back, buyers would need substantial volume injection and a push above $0.000005, until then, SHIB balances will keep growing substantially on exchanges and causing a pressure spike at some point in time in the future.

#5227. 'Don’t Be Silly': Satoshi Associate Dismisses Bitcoin Quantum Collapse Claims

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin developers have renewed the discussion about the long-term security of the network amid the development of quantum computing ("Q-Day"). The trigger was an interview StarkWare CEO Eli Ben-Sasson gave to journalist Pete Rizzo.

According to Ben-Sasson, without the implementation of a "quantum fix," Bitcoin will lose its leadership, and its market value will collapse due to the physical limitations of current computing systems.

Criticism of these forecasts came from Adam Back, the creator of Hashcash, co-founder of Blockstream and one of the cryptographers mentioned by Satoshi Nakamoto in the Bitcoin whitepaper. On the social network X, Back described the panic expectations with the phrase: "c'mon guys, don't be silly and alarmist."

According to Back, the risks are exaggerated, since algorithms for protection against quantum threats are already at the protocol planning stage. He pointed out that Ben-Sasson's own company, StarkWare, builds its products on STARK technology: zero-knowledge proofs that run on cryptographic hash functions.

In the Bitcoin technical community, hash functions are considered the main security factor. According to theoretical models, quantum computers running Shor's algorithm are capable of breaking the network's current asymmetric cryptography (ECDSA and Schnorr).

However, this method is ineffective against hash functions (including SHA-256). Grover's algorithm gives quantum systems only a quadratic speedup in brute-force search, which, according to Back, is fully neutralized by Bitcoin's standard security settings.

As an alternative scenario, Satoshi Nakamoto's associate cited data on the community's current technical developments, which by late 2026 have moved into the testing phase:

SHRINCS protocol: A modified hash-based signature (Shrunken SPHINCS). According to Blockstream developers, they optimized it for Bitcoin's bandwidth limits.

Implementation status: An official draft Bitcoin Improvement Proposal (BIP) has been published for review. According to the project's roadmap, the technology is undergoing stress tests on the Liquid sidechain.

Critics of post-quantum cryptography often name data volume as the main problem. Quantum-resistant signatures are bulkier than standard 64-byte Schnorr keys, which, according to a number of researchers, could overload the blockchain and raise fees.

Back argues that the infrastructure is capable of scaling through data aggregation. According to his position, batch verification methods and STARK hash proofs make it possible to compress thousands of signatures into one compact package. According to supporters of this approach, the solution will preserve the network's speed and protect its decentralization.

The current discussion demonstrates the existence of two polar views: part of the industry considers the quantum threat an urgent problem, while Bitcoin core developers speak of planned testing of the protective infrastructure, which is proceeding in parallel with the development of quantum capabilities.

#5225. SEC ruling lifts Canton Coin 6% as a16z sees pathway out of securities laws

Andreessen Horowitz's Miles Jennings hailed the regulator's digital commodity classification as a breakthrough that could extend beyond Canton's institutional blockchain.

Canton Coin rose more than 6% after US securities regulators recognized the blockchain's native token as a digital commodity.

The token traded at $0.09892, according to CryptoSlate's data, as investors assessed a Nov. 3 decision that removes a significant regulatory obstacle for the institutional blockchain network.

The Securities and Exchange Commission's (SEC) Division of Corporation Finance said it would not recommend enforcement action against certain network participants that earn newly minted Canton Coin (CC) for operating infrastructure and applications without registering those activities under federal securities laws.

The decision drew praise from Miles Jennings, head of policy and general counsel at Andreessen Horowitz's a16z Frontier, who argued that the ruling establishes a broader regulatory pathway for blockchain projects.

“Incredible,” Jennings wrote on X, pointing to the SEC's March token classification framework and subsequent guidance.

He added that the developments had created a clear pathway out of securities laws, arguing that network tokens should be treated as digital commodities.

His assessment carries particular commercial significance for Andreessen Horowitz. The venture capital firm's a16z crypto fund led a $355 million financing round for Canton developer Digital Asset in June, alongside financial institutions including BNP Paribas, HSBC and Citadel Securities.

Under the arrangement submitted by Digital Asset's lawyers, three groups can earn newly created tokens: Super Validators that operate synchronization infrastructure, Validators that process and support transactions, and application providers that build services connected to the network.

Participants receive rewards based on measurable contributions through automated minting rounds. Canton Coin also serves as payment for transaction fees, with tokens used for those fees subsequently burned.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The SEC staff accepted the company's representations that the token derives its value from network activity and market supply and demand, rather than expectations of profits generated by the managerial efforts of others.

Another consideration was Canton's governance structure, which the company described as lacking a central controlling party.

According to Digital Asset's submission, neither the developer nor the Canton Foundation can independently change the network's economic parameters. Such changes require approval from at least two-thirds of Super Validator Node Operators.

These characteristics align with the SEC's March 17 interpretation, which distinguished digital commodities from securities and addressed the treatment of crypto mining, staking and other network activities.

The agency subsequently expanded that framework through frequently asked questions, including clarifications issued Nov. 2 concerning decentralized governance arrangements.

Canton's decision provides a concrete example of how blockchain developers can apply those guidelines to existing networks, potentially giving other projects a model for structuring token-based infrastructure incentives.

However, Jennings' broader interpretation goes beyond the specific assurance the SEC staff provided.

The no-action position covers Canton's described minting arrangements and does not automatically exempt other token distributions or investment products from securities laws. The division also emphasized that its response expresses an enforcement position rather than a binding legal conclusion.

Other blockchain developers seeking similar treatment would therefore need to establish that their network operations, governance arrangements and token distribution mechanisms satisfy the relevant regulatory criteria.

#5224. North Korea Is Laundering Binance Hack Loot at One New Wallet Every 25 Seconds

North Korea Is Laundering Binance Hack Loot at One New Wallet Every 25 Seconds

North Korean hackers are moving the $6.4 billion they stole from Binance at a blistering clip: roughly one new crypto wallet every 25 seconds.

In under four days, the attacker has spread the funds across about 12,000 wallets on 11 blockchains in more than 40,000 transactions, according to an analysis published Monday by blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

But stablecoin issuers moved even faster. About $2.85 billion, or 44% of the haul, has been frozen or seized, compared with roughly 3% after North Korea’s $1.5 billion hack of Bybit in February 2025.

Speed mattered because stablecoins made up $3.46 billion, or 54%, of the haul, and their issuers can freeze them. Tether froze the stolen USDT on Ethereum 37 minutes after it reached the attacker’s wallet, and Circle froze all $823 million in stolen USDC before any of it could be swapped, Elliptic found.

Before the freezes landed, the hacker swapped 434 million USDT for ETH on decentralized exchanges such as Uniswap, borrowed 19,762 ETH on lending protocol Aave against another 59.9 million USDT, and traded 234 million USDT for Tron’s TRX token. In all, about $786 million in stablecoins got away.

Attackers began draining Binance’s hot wallets late Thursday. The FBI has since attributed the theft to TraderTraitor, a North Korean state-linked hacking group, and published hundreds of linked addresses.

Binance, which has pledged to cover all losses, reopened withdrawals Sunday and saw about $3.64 billion in net outflows in the first nine and a half hours.

The laundering that followed has been relentless. On Tron, which became the main route, activity peaked at about 1,300 transactions in a single hour.

That pace fueled speculation that AI agents were doing the work. Elliptic disagreed, pointing to fixed timing, set amounts, blind retries and pauses that resemble human working hours. The firm wrote that while the laundering is clearly automated, “nothing we have seen shows an AI agent making decisions.”

About $3.24 billion remains in addresses linked to the theft, mostly ETH and the 18,270 stolen Bitcoin, which haven’t moved since the day of the attack. Elliptic believes those funds are very likely still held by North Korea, while money already being laundered may have passed to Chinese money-laundering networks.

Only a negligible amount appears to have been cashed out. About $16 million has also flowed to addresses holding proceeds of September’s Bitget exploit.

Based on the Bybit playbook, Elliptic expects the attacker to swap its ETH for Bitcoin in small batches and cash out through over-the-counter brokers over a period of months. Further freezes will be harder, the firm said, since ETH and Bitcoin can’t be frozen. The best remaining chances lie with centralized services, where the funds will eventually have to surface.

The Binance theft caps a punishing stretch for crypto security. BTCPay Server urged merchants to switch off their servers amid reports of hot wallet attacks on Monday, and Brazilian exchange Mercado Bitcoin halted withdrawals after a $40 million hack. It’s unclear whether either incident is connected to the Binance attack.

#5222. New Project Backed by Ripple Co-Founder Faces Heavy XRP Community Backlash

A new blockchain project, Yellow, that previously attracted $10 million in personal investment from Ripple co-founder Chris Larsen has faced massive criticism from members of the XRP community immediately after the listing of its token. Observers’ attention was drawn to asset-selling operations from a wallet that is linked to the developers.

The project’s token was launched simultaneously on the Ethereum and XRP Ledger (XRPL) networks. Due to the absence of a direct technical bridge between the blockchains and high initial demand, the asset’s price on XRPL turned out to be significantly higher than on the Ethereum network.

During this period, tokens worth about 20,000 XRP were sold on the XRPL market from a wallet associated with the startup’s team. Project representative Burak Cem explained in private chats that these transactions were “team wallet arbitraging,” necessary to reduce the emerging price premium and align the rates.

The team’s actions provoked a mixed reaction in the community. Kaj Leroy, a developer popular in the XRP Ledger ecosystem, expressed doubt about the technical possibility of conducting full-fledged arbitrage trades without a working cross-chain bridge.

According to critics, such operations on the first day of trading effectively amount to “liquidity extraction” at the expense of early retail buyers.

The incident developed in the context of a public discussion started by influencer JC Rivera , who reproached the XRP community for systematic skepticism toward new startups. In response, opponents led by Kaj Leroy justified the community’s wariness with the new project’s on-chain data.

@JcRivera @xrplto because they dumped 20k xrp worth on their holders like instantly and said its for arbitrage?🤣 onboarding people to extract this obviously is not the way bro

In their opinion, external teams often use rhetoric about “onboarding” users, while their actions lead to locking in quick profits at the expense of XRP holders’ liquidity.

At the moment, the project, which plans to launch clearing solutions for AI agents, has not provided any additional official comments on the situation. The local community’s level of trust in the new startup remains under pressure.

Deposits of about $6 billion offset just over half the withdrawals, leaving roughly $5 billion in net outflows over the same period, according to data released by Binance on Tuesday.

#5219. Tom Lee Calls Bottom on AI Stock Selloff, Citing Tight Supply and Crypto Resilience

Tom Lee Calls Bottom on AI Stock Selloff, Citing Tight Supply and Crypto Resilience

Fundstrat co-founder and Bitmine chairman Tom Lee says the AI stock selloff has reached its bottom, arguing that demand remains constrained by supply and that crypto’s response to a multibillion-dollar hack suggests investors have already done much of their selling.

“I think we're at the bottom,” Lee said in a CNBC interview on Squawk Box. His call follows a decline that has spread beyond individual AI companies: large technology stocks also sit inside the funds many people use to invest for retirement. (Disclosure: Tom Lee is one of several investors in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

Lee acknowledged how difficult it is to identify the point where prices stop falling. “Bottoms are dangerous because you don't know how much more downside you have at the bottom,” he said.

His case rests on a distinction between an industry struggling to expand fast enough and one that has built more capacity than customers need.

“The market for AI is still very tight. It's not an overcapacity market,” Lee said. Constraints on spending, including political opposition, are slowing growth without signaling that the boom has peaked, he argued.

The distinction matters for companies building the infrastructure behind AI, including data centers, the facilities that house the computers running these systems. A shortage can leave customers waiting, but it can also prevent suppliers from turning orders into revenue.

Intel offered a particularly sharp example on Friday, when its shares plunged nearly 30% to $65.76 after a disappointing fourth-quarter forecast. Its data center and AI revenue had risen 78% from a year earlier to $7.3 billion, yet manufacturing constraints limited its ability to meet demand.

Intel projected quarterly revenue of $15 billion to $16 billion, with the midpoint about $1.6 billion below analysts’ expectations. Its weaker PC business added pressure, showing why healthy AI demand can coexist with a falling share price.

Lee’s second argument looks outside the stock market. He pointed to crypto’s relatively limited response to the Binance security breach, which the exchange initially estimated had an impact of about $6.3 billion.

“If crypto is a leading indicator for risk, you've already seen people sort of washed out,” he said. In his reading, a market that absorbs bad news without a much larger decline may have fewer sellers left to push prices down.

Bitcoin did fall about 4% to roughly $76,400 on Friday, and more than $1.3 billion in positions funded with borrowed money were forcibly closed. U.S. spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds, which hold Bitcoin and trade like stocks, also shed $643.2 million that day, their largest daily outflow of October.

That said, a 4% drop can happen even during bull runs and it is considered a relatively normal occurrence among crypto traders.

Binance said on Friday that about $3 billion of the affected assets had been frozen and pledged to cover losses using its emergency reserve and company funds.

#5217. Bitcoin rebounds after Democratic sweep despite three days of ETF outflows

US spot Bitcoin ETFs lost $1,157 million over the three trading sessions ending Nov. 3, leaving one analyst's $90,000 recovery forecast dependent on returning demand.

Bitcoin rebounded above $78,000 on Wednesday after Democrats won both chambers of Congress, despite three consecutive trading sessions of US spot ETF withdrawals.

The Democratic victories in Tuesday's midterm elections dealt President Donald Trump a political setback. In a pre-election Bloomberg survey, respondents had forecast lower year-end Bitcoin prices under that outcome than under Republican or divided control.

The survey's median year-end forecast was $76,450 under a Democratic sweep, compared with $84,100 under divided congressional control and $90,800 if Republicans retained both chambers.

Ryan Kirkley, co-founder and CEO of blockchain infrastructure company Global Settlement Network, argued ahead of the election that Bitcoin could recover toward $85,000-$90,000 regardless of the outcome. His forecast depended on ETF flows stabilizing, liquidity improving and confidence returning to the broader economy.

“My view is that investors are probably giving Washington too much credit for Bitcoin's direction,” Kirkley said.

Bitcoin briefly fell below $76,000 as election results emerged before recovering above $78,000 on Wednesday.

The election followed a sharp deterioration in cryptocurrency social sentiment.

According to blockchain analysis firm Santiment, Bitcoin's bullish-to-bearish social commentary ratio fell from about 1.8 on Oct. 20 to 0.51 as Democrats moved toward control of Congress.

The decline accelerated after Binance suffered a roughly $6 billion security breach in late October, when the ratio briefly collapsed to 0.37 before recovering for several days.

Santiment attributed the renewed pessimism to perceptions that Republicans are more supportive of cryptocurrencies, with the prospect of Democratic control of Congress adding to concerns about the industry's political future.

However, the analytics firm suggested that the extreme pessimism could create conditions for a contrarian recovery, particularly if Bitcoin rebounds after traders have positioned themselves for further losses.

Analysts at Bitunix warned that Democratic control could reshape Washington's approach to cryptocurrency regulation, potentially shifting congressional priorities from advancing industry legislation toward investigations of conflicts of interest, exchange oversight and broader financial risks.

The industry's flagship market-structure legislation, the CLARITY Act, had already encountered resistance before the election.

On Sept. 15, the Senate rejected a procedural motion to advance the legislation by 49 votes to 50, falling short of the 60 votes required.

The bill seeks to establish clearer boundaries between Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) and Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) oversight, an effort that crypto companies have long argued would reduce legal uncertainty.

Bitunix cautioned that the incoming Democratic majority could complicate negotiations, although some Democratic lawmakers have supported efforts to establish comprehensive digital asset rules.

The new Congress will not take office until January 2027, so the immediate market impact will depend largely on how investors price in future policy changes.

Bitunix also identified a potential counterweight to the political uncertainty. Declining Treasury yields and a weaker US dollar following the election could ease financial conditions and support risk assets, including Bitcoin.

Kirkley's bullish forecast faces a more immediate challenge from withdrawals from Bitcoin exchange-traded funds.

According to Farside Investors, US-listed spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded approximately $1,157 million in net withdrawals across the three consecutive trading sessions ending Nov. 3.

The outflows began with $643 million on Oct. 30, followed by $399 million on Nov. 2 and another $116 million on election day. BlackRock's IBIT accounted for $95 million of the Nov. 3 redemptions.

The withdrawals add to selling pressure from existing Bitcoin holders, many of whom acquired their assets at prices above current market levels.

In its Oct. 28 market report, Glassnode estimated that about 4 million BTC held by long-term investors last moved between $85,000 and $118,000.

Some of those investors could seek to recover their initial investments as Bitcoin approaches those levels, creating a potential wave of selling near Kirkley's year-end target.

Glassnode identified $84,000-$85,000 as a particularly difficult resistance zone, having repeatedly rejected Bitcoin rallies throughout 2026.

Before reaching that level, Bitcoin would need to reclaim the aggregate cost basis of US spot Bitcoin ETF holdings, estimated at approximately $81,500 in Glassnode's October report.

A sustained move above $87,500, accompanied by repeated ETF inflows and rising trading volumes, would offer stronger evidence that buyers can absorb the supply waiting overhead.

Glassnode placed the short-term holder cost basis near $75,800. In its Nov. 3 outlook, 21Shares identified a broader support area around $75,800-$76,200, with the 200-day moving average near $73,000 below it.

The next test will come from ETF flows after the election, particularly whether investors reverse three consecutive sessions of withdrawals or continue selling as Bitcoin attempts to reclaim $80,000.

#5215. Claude-Built Linux Exploit Targets Bug That Ubuntu Still Hasn't Patched

Claude-Built Linux Exploit Targets Bug That Ubuntu Still Hasn't Patched

Another day, another exploit from ChatGPT or Claude that gives us the chills.

Anthropic says its AI wrote a working attack that lets a stranger take full control of a Linux server over the internet, no password needed. The bug behind it, CVE-2026-98432, has been hiding in Linux since version 4.4 and scores 9.8 out of 10 for severity.

Linux runs countless servers, and Ubuntu is one of its most popular flavors. Ubuntu's own tracker still lists every version from 16.04 to 26.10 as vulnerable.

Anthropic described the attack on Oct. 27 in a research post covering five targets: Chrome, Safari, Android, Windows, and Linux. Claude wrote the Linux exploit after initial prompting, using an unreleased internal model.

The attack takes less than an hour to find a hidden spot in the computer's memory, then five more minutes to run a command with the highest level of access, known as root.

The flaw works like reading a sticky note after someone already shredded it. One part of the system throws away a piece of information while another part is still using it, per AlmaLinux. A specially crafted message that opens an internet connection can set that off.

Failing costs the attacker almost nothing. "An enabling property of this race condition is that a lost race only fails the handshake without crashing the whole system," Anthropic wrote, "so a determined attacker can retry indefinitely until they succeed."

Anthropic's version needs a server using a feature called multipath TCP, which lets one connection travel over several routes at once. Programs written in Go have switched it on by default since Go 1.24, per the Go Security Team.

But the bug is not limited to that feature. "While the demonstrated exploit requires the target system to expose an MPTCP listener, the underlying bug is not specific to MPTCP," the Go Security Team wrote.

Red Hat released fixes for RHEL 8, 9 and 10 on Sunday, including RHSA-2026:93412. Debian, SUSE, Oracle Linux and Amazon Linux have also shipped fixes, at least for some versions.

For anyone who can't update yet, Red Hat published a temporary firewall rule. It "reduces exposure to this vulnerability but cannot fully prevent it," Red Hat says in its security bulletin. A full working solution is still under development.

Anthropic's earlier Claude Mythos model helped Mozilla find 271 bugs in Firefox, as Decrypt reported in April.

Ubuntu's tracker shows fixes in progress for 22.04, 24.04, 26.04 and 26.10. Versions 20.04, 18.04 and 16.04 show only "Vulnerable," and the tracker gives no release date for any fix.

#5213. Binance Reopens Withdrawals as Hack Recovery Enters Second Week

Binance has reopened withdrawals after its $6.3 billion North Korea hack, covered 95% of losses, and has reserves for the remaining 5%, with post-mortem reviews to stay open.

Binance restarted customer withdrawals on Sunday, four days after hackers drained roughly $6.3 billion from its hot wallets in the largest crypto theft on record.

The exchange reopened in stages over the weekend, starting with bitcoin and expanding to 15 networks. The move marked the first real test of whether the platform could absorb a loss of that size without freezing its customers out for longer.

The recovery push began Saturday. In its third recovery update, Binance said it had replenished 90% of the impacted amount with company funds. The exchange drew on part of its Secure Asset Fund for Users, a portion of its futures insurance fund and its corporate treasury to buy replacement tokens.

The futures fund contribution drew particular attention, since Binance described the remaining balance as a regulatory requirement. Binance also published a new proof-of-reserves snapshot dated October 31, giving users a baseline to check their balances against.

Sunday’s reopening began at 06:00 UTC with BTC. Binance then added ETH, BNB Smart Chain, Solana, XRP, Dogecoin, Tron, Polygon, Avalanche, Arbitrum, Base, Optimism, Zcash, TON and Sui. Between 06:00 and 15:30 UTC, the exchange processed about $4.65 billion in withdrawals against roughly $1.01 billion in deposits.

That produced a net outflow near $3.64 billion, equal to about 2.1% of the $171 billion in assets Binance reported in its late-October proof of reserves.

Deposits stayed open throughout the rollout, and the exchange said temporary delays were possible during the initial surge in volume.

Binance said its Sunday update raised coverage to 95% of the impacted amount. The remaining 5% sits in less liquid tokens outside the top ten assets on its proof-of-reserves list. Sourcing those tokens at the required scale takes more time, and the exchange said it is working with industry partners and over-the-counter desks to complete the purchases. Binance has committed to covering the full amount, and it has repeated that users will not bear the financial impact of the theft.

On Monday, Binance said the full zero-knowledge proof for the October 31 snapshot is complete. Users holding BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB, SOL, USDC, XRP, USD1, RLUSD or ZEC can run the exchange’s verifier to confirm their balances are backed at least 1:1.

The verifier is open source, and the exchange has pointed users to a step-by-step guide.

The exchange plans another snapshot covering more tokens on November 4, with a proof expected to take 40 to 50 hours to generate. A further withdrawal phase is scheduled for Tuesday, and the exchange said it would share details once the rollout is ready.

On the law enforcement front, the FBI has attributed the attack to North Korea, and the U.S. Secret Service is working with Circle to trace and freeze funds. Tether reported about $1.57 billion in USDT frozen, and Circle said it had frozen more than $800 million in USDC in coordination with authorities.

The market reacted sharply to the breach. Bitcoin fell about 5% to below $77,000 in the hours after the attack, and shares of Coinbase,Robinhood, Strategy, and Strive all declined as investors weighed the risk of exchange-level losses across the sector.

Binance has said it found additional points of breach beyond the initial compromise, which it originally attributed to a single engineer’s laptop. The exchange has not yet published a full post-mortem explaining how the attackers prepared and executed the operation, which some estimates suggest took at least 36 hours of groundwork.

The destination of roughly $3.3 billion in stolen funds that were not frozen has also not been fully explained, and the exchange has not detailed what it expects to recover from that pool. Investigators continue to track wallet activity across ten blockchains.

#5212. Why rising XRP whale counts are a fake bullish signal

A split of XRP stolen from Binance created six large accounts, showing how wallet counts can grow through transfers without establishing fresh buying.

A redistribution of XRP stolen from Binance created six new accounts above the one-million-token threshold on Nov. 2, showing how large-wallet counts can grow through transfers alone.

Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery traced the split from one receiving account into seven new accounts, with six clearing the million-token threshold.

An address tally can increase through the movement of existing holdings, making its interpretation important before drawing a bullish conclusion.

Binance's Nov. 3 report said five of its hot wallets sent 233.46 million XRP to an attacker-controlled address between 20:05 and 20:09 UTC on Oct. 29.

Bitquery's address list shows that five Nov. 2 recipients received 40,001,000 XRP each. Another received 33,455,920 XRP, alongside the small seventh account.

For a raw count of addresses holding at least one million XRP, six qualifying recipients replacing one qualifying sender would mean a net increase of five. That calculation requires the sender to have been counted beforehand, all six recipients to qualify afterward, and consistent address coverage and observation cutoffs.

A single pool of stolen XRP can appear as several large balances, without requiring another buyer. Distinguishing redistribution from accumulation involves tracing the tokens' source and identifying which addresses belong together.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

In an Oct. 29 analysis, CryptoQuant analyst Amr Taha reported that smaller transfers' share of XRP exchange outflows rose from 17% on Sept. 30 to 33.4% on Oct. 29.

That change does not establish a corresponding increase in independently held balances, and transfer size alone cannot establish whether the recipient is a new investor.

CryptoQuant separately reported Binance's XRP spot-to-futures trading-volume ratio at 33.44% on Oct. 28, meaning $33.44 of spot turnover for every $100 traded in futures.

Bitquery reported no onward transactions from the seven XRP recipients as of Nov. 3.

Binance said in its Nov. 3 update that it would absorb the attack's financial impact and that 98% of impacted token assets had been replenished as of Oct. 31.

For investors assessing XRP's recovery, the traced split puts the origin of large balances ahead of their number.

Rising wallet counts become more informative when they connect to new buying and independently held balances. Here, a transfer of existing stolen tokens is sufficient to explain the creation of six large accounts.

#5211. Mystery $106 Million Crypto Theft Hits EU Entity Days After Binance Hack

Mystery $106 Million Crypto Theft Hits EU Entity Days After Binance Hack

Another nine-figure theft has hit crypto, just days after the biggest heist in the industry’s history.

An unnamed entity in the European Union is suspected of losing more than $106 million from several of its accounts on centralized exchanges on Sunday, pseudonymous onchain investigator ZachXBT said Monday in a post to his Telegram channel.

The funds were withdrawn as USDC and USDT stablecoins by what he called an “illicit actor.”

ZachXBT published 11 Ethereum addresses tied to the theft.

Instant exchanges let users quickly swap one cryptocurrency for another, often with little identity verification, while Monero hides senders, receivers and amounts, making it a popular tool for obscuring stolen funds.

Swapping out of USDC and USDT also matters because the companies behind those stablecoins can freeze them.

ZachXBT said the culprit remains unidentified, but that he could say “with a high degree of confidence” that it isn’t Lazarus Group, citing multiple laundering indicators. Lazarus is the umbrella name often used for North Korea’s state-backed hackers. ZachXBT didn’t name the victim.

That sets the theft apart from the record-setting $6.4 billion Binance hack, which the FBI has attributed to TraderTraitor, a North Korean hacking group.

In that case, stablecoin issuers moved within minutes. Tether froze stolen USDT on Ethereum in 37 minutes, and Circle froze all $823 million in stolen USDC, helping lock up about 44% of the haul, according to blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.

North Korea’s launderers have since spread the remaining funds across about 12,000 wallets. It’s unclear whether any funds in the EU case have been frozen.

The incident adds to an already unprecedented week for crypto security. Beyond the record-breaking Binance hack, Brazilian exchange Mercado Bitcoin just today reported halting withdrawals after a $40 million hack of its own. Likewise today, BTCPay Server told merchants to switch off their servers amid reports of hot wallet attacks.

#5207. Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin Freezes Deposits and Withdrawals Due to Ongoing Security Incident, Losses Estimated Near $40 Million

Brazil’s largest crypto exchange Mercado Bitcoin says customers suffered no losses, but it has not said how much was taken or when it will reopen.

Brazil’s largest cryptocurrency exchange kept deposits and withdrawals suspended on Monday after a security incident in one of its systems. The shutdown left customers unable to send funds into or out of the platform while the company worked to restore operations.

Mercado Bitcoin said in a statement posted to X that it had identified and contained the incident. The company said customers suffered no losses and that no data had leaked. It described the suspension as a precaution. Its teams are investigating, tracing assets and coordinating with authorities, the company said. According to Bloomberg, the company said it would absorb all losses.

The company has not said which system was breached, how much was taken or when it will reopen. Its current statements gave no total loss figure and no timetable.

Mercado Bitcoin has since published destination addresses tied to transactions from the incident, covering Bitcoin, Ethereum-compatible networks, Solana, Stellar and Tron. It said it shared the addresses for identification and tracking purposes. Blockchain security firm Hypernative has put preliminary estimates of suspicious transfers above $40 million across several blockchains. The exchange has not confirmed that figure.

Other analysts are citing higher figures and that the sweeping is ongoing.

Stablecoin issuer Tether has entered the response. Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said on X at 10:06 a.m. ET that the company had frozen more than 4.5 million USDT linked to the incident. Tether invested $20 million in Mercado Bitcoin back in July, when it said the exchange served 4.5 million users.

The incident comes days after one of the largest crypto thefts in recent memory. On Oct. 29, attackers reached $6.3 billion in assets at Binance after compromising an engineer’s laptop and gaining access to internal systems used to process withdrawals.

Binance said it found no evidence that private keys were exposed. By Nov. 1, it had replaced 95 percent of the stolen assets and begun restoring withdrawals in phases.

On the law enforcement front, the FBI has attributed the attack to North Korea, and the U.S. Secret Service is working with Circle to trace and freeze funds. Tether reported about $1.57 billion in USDT frozen, and Circle said it had frozen more than $800 million in USDC in coordination with authorities.

Binance has said it found additional points of breach beyond the initial compromise, which it originally attributed to a single engineer’s laptop. The exchange has not yet published a full post-mortem explaining how the attackers prepared and executed the operation, which some estimates suggest took at least 36 hours of groundwork.

The destination of roughly $3.3 billion in stolen funds that were not frozen has also not been fully explained, and the exchange has not detailed what it expects to recover from that pool. Investigators continue to track wallet activity across ten blockchains.

#5202. Kim Jong-Un’s sister rejects FBI claims linking North Korea to record $6 billion Binance hack: reports

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

On Monday, Yonhap News Agency reported that hackers from North Korea were not behind the over $6 billion hack of Binance, citing the sister of North Korea's leader Kim Jong-Un.

A North Korean official has denied the country was behind the largest crypto hack on record, despite onchain investigators and the FBI attributing the $6 billion exploit to North Korean hackers.

On Monday, Yonhap News Agency reported that Kim Yo-jong, the sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong-Un and a senior government official, rejected the FBI's assessment that North Korea was responsible for last week's Binance hack.

"The FBI is trying to brand the DPRK as a 'cyber criminal state', claiming that it obtained information that a hacking organization 'supported' by the DPRK was involved in the incident," Kim said in a statement, according to Yonhap.

The FBI did not immediately respond to a request for comment from The Block.

Ahead of the attack last week, an attacker deployed and tested malicious code on three wallets before later executing the attack on Binance on Thursday. On Ethereum, the attacker used EIP-7702 to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper controlled by the attacker. It prepared 50 pre-signed authorizations to ensure one would remain valid when executed, according to TRM Labs.

Three firms, including TRM Labs, attributed the attack to North Korean bad actors. Then, over the weekend, the FBI confirmed that North Korea was responsible for the hack. In its release, the FBI referred to this specific North Korean malicious cyber activity as "TraderTraitor."

Earlier on Monday, Binance published its first set of proof of reserves and said it replenished about 95% of the assets affected by the hack with its own funds.
