#5200. Bitcoin Firm Strive Climbs Up the Treasury Ranks With Another Big Buy

Bitcoin Firm Strive Climbs Up the Treasury Ranks With Another Big Buy

Strive, the Dallas-based company co-founded by Vivek Ramaswamy, now holds 43,124 Bitcoin after buying 124 BTC more last week. That stash is now worth roughly $3.3 billion, and good for about 0.2% of the 21 million coins that will ever exist.

That leaves it fourth among publicly traded companies, 390 coins behind Twenty One Capital (which holds 43,514 BTC) and 876 BTC behind Metaplanet (which holds 44,000 BTC), according to Bitcoin Treasuries data.

At Bitcoin’s price of about $77,000, those gaps are worth roughly $30 million and $67 million, respectively.

Strive is a Bitcoin treasury company—a publicly listed firm that keeps Bitcoin on its balance sheet, so anyone with a brokerage account can get exposure by buying its stock. Michael Saylor’s Strategy popularized the playbook back in 2020, and several other companies followed suit, including Strive.

Its coins cost an average of $87,785 each, or $3.8 billion in total, against a market value of $3.5 billion on Oct. 23, per its prospectus. Bitcoin has slipped since, so the pile is now worth a bit less than Strive paid for it.

The latest purchase covers Oct. 24 through Oct. 30 and was disclosed today in an SEC filing, the report US-listed companies must submit when something material happens. Strive paid about $10 million, an average of $80,526 per Bitcoin including fees, CEO Matt Cole said on X.

Strive acquired 124 $BTC for $10M at an average cost of $80,526 per bitcoin, bringing total holdings to ₿43,124.⁰⁰- Existing cash funded most purchases⁰- SATS ATM used for first time, representing 100% of weekly ATM proceeds⁰- Amplification Ratio: 62.7%⁰⁰$ASST $SATA $SATS pic.twitter.com/rFrS6y4AVc

— Matt Cole (@ColeMacro) November 2, 2026

Most of the money came from existing cash, which fell from $504.4 million to $490.4 million over the week. Strive also raised money for the first time by selling shares of a new security called SATS on the open market, Cole said, and that made up all of the week’s fundraising. It still holds 505,000 shares of Strategy’s preferred stock, known as STRC, worth about $50 million.

SATS is a preferred stock—a type of share that pays a fixed dividend before common shareholders see anything. Strive sold 7 million of them at $90 each in an October offering that raised about $630 million, and they carry a 15% annual dividend on a $100 stated value, paid every business day from Nov. 9.

On Oct. 26, Strive opened an at-the-market program—a way to sell new shares in small batches at the going price—for up to $1 billion more SATS. The prospectus says Strive expects to pay the dividends mainly by raising more capital, including selling more shares. The company has set aside 18 months of dividends in a reserve and reported no debt as of Oct. 23.

Strive’s amplification ratio, the preferred stock and debt it carries measured against the value of its Bitcoin, stood at 62.7%, or about $62.70 of obligations for every $100 of Bitcoin.

The company’s CEO has said ending 2026 as the second-largest public Bitcoin holder is possible. “I wouldn’t say that’s my base case,” Cole said on the One Chair podcast in September, “but I would say it’s in the possible set of cards.”

Saylor’s Strategy holds 849,327 Bitcoin, nearly 20 times Strive’s pile.

Twenty One Capital still holds the same 43,514 Bitcoin it had when co-founder Jack Mallers stepped down as CEO in July. His exit came as a plan by Tether to merge three Bitcoin businesses collapsed. The stock had fallen more than 80% from its highs, and his successor, Raphael Zagury, has said Twenty One should be judged on cash flow and capital discipline.

Metaplanet, a Tokyo-listed company that pivoted from hotels to Bitcoin in 2024, holds 44,000 Bitcoin after overtaking mining firm MARA in April. It has a stated goal of 100,000 by the end of 2026.

At last week’s pace of 124 Bitcoin, Strive would pass Twenty One in four weeks and Metaplanet in eight, assuming neither company buys more.

#5197. Ark Invest buys $3.4 million in Coinbase, adds Circle and Robinhood as stocks trade lower

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Cathie Wood's Ark Invest bought $3.4 million worth of Coinbase shares, $2.6 million worth of Circle, and $645,820 worth of Robinhood on Monday.

Cathie Wood-led investment firm Ark Invest increased its holdings in Coinbase, Circle, and Robinhood through its exchange-traded funds on Monday, as all three stocks closed lower.

According to Ark's latest trading disclosure, the company purchased 24,790 Coinbase Coinbase Global, Inc. (COIN) shares, worth $3.4 million, through its ARKK, ARKW, and ARKF ETFs. Coinbase shares closed down 3.98% at $137.83 on Monday.

Ark bought 35,657 shares of Circle Circle Internet Group, Inc. (CRCL), worth $2.6 million, as the stock dropped 0.6%. It also added 7,703 Robinhood Robinhood Markets, Inc. (HOOD) shares, worth $645,820 based on Monday's closing price.

Meanwhile, the firm sold 104,172 shares of Bullish Bullish (BLSH), worth $2.3 million. The crypto exchange's shares fell 5.69% to close at $22.54.

Ark's moves came days after Coinbase reported strong third-quarter earnings last week. The company's Q3 net income rose 66% year on year to $719 million from $433 million in the same period last year. It generated $1.22 billion in total revenue, broadly flat from the previous quarter but down 35% year over year.

Notably, Coinbase's prediction markets business surpassed $200 million in annualized revenue, doubling from roughly $100 million in the previous quarter. Bernstein analysts said Friday that the growth in the company's prediction markets and subscription and services revenue "helped offset the softer crypto trading activity."

Circle is scheduled to report its Q3 earnings on Nov. 4. Consensus estimates call for revenue of $788.17 million and EPS of $0.37, according to TradingView. Robinhood reported a 20% jump in crypto revenue from the previous quarter to $120 million, offsetting a decline in crypto trading revenue, according to its results released last week. Meanwhile, Bullish is expected to report Q3 earnings on Nov. 18.

#5196. Nvidia, Nike Join 50 Companies Blocking OKX–ICE Stock Token Push

Fifty companies, including Nvidia, Microsoft and JPMorgan, have blocked OKX and ICE from tokenizing their shares under the SEC’s new Innovation Exemption.

Fifty public companies have now objected to having their shares tokenized on OKXICE, the joint venture between crypto exchange OKX and New York Stock Exchange parent Intercontinental Exchange (ICE), as the venue’s first 30-day notice window closes Tuesday.

The venue’s Oct. 30 notice added 12 objectors, including Nvidia, Costco, Nike, Micron and Reddit. They join Microsoft, Alphabet, JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Walmart, Broadcom, Verizon and Intel, among others. Of the 62 companies OKXICE initially proposed to tokenize, 43, or about 69%, have objected, according to a CoinDesk tally.

The list has grown fast: nine objectors on Oct. 15, 16 on Oct. 19, 38 on Oct. 28 and 50 two days later.

The objections stem from the SEC’s five-year “Innovation Exemption,” issued Sept. 17, which lets tokenized securities venues (TSVs) trade blockchain versions of U.S. stocks through automated market makers without registering as exchanges. When an unaffiliated third party tokenizes a company’s stock, the venue must notify the issuer, which then has 30 days to object in writing. Silence counts as consent, and the order offers no way to object after the window closes.

OKXICE plans 24/7 trading of tokens backed one-for-one by shares, paired against stablecoins such as USDC in permissioned pools on OKX’s X Layer blockchain.

The objecting companies have not given reasons. JPMorgan said it remains encouraged by tokenization but wants to be thoughtful about how its shares are represented and traded. “Given how easy the opt-out is, the list of objectors is likely to keep growing,” Olivia Vande Woude, head of tokenization at Ava Labs, said.

The fight has also taken on a competitive edge. An executive at a Nasdaq-listed company told Crypto in America that a Nasdaq representative urged the company to object and offered a call to explain why. Nasdaq declined to comment on the claim but reiterated that TSVs are not the best path to tokenization.

Nasdaq argues that venues outside the National Market System could fragment liquidity and lack real-time best-price display and consolidated-tape reporting. It is building its own issuer-centric Nasdaq Equity Token with Kraken, targeted for the second quarter of 2027.

A full-page Wall Street Journal ad urging CEOs to opt out, headlined “YOUR STOCK. THEIR TOKENS. YOUR PROBLEM.,” linked to Nasdaq’s FAQ via QR code. Conservative-leaning nonprofit Common Sense Leadership Fund paid for it. Nasdaq said it had no connection to the ad and never funded the group.

OKXICE co-chair and former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo has called the objections a “natural response.”

“We are at the very beginning of the rollout of fundamentally different market structure,” he said last week.

For context, a tokenized stock is a blockchain token backed one-for-one by a real share, which a third party holds. OKXICE lets it trade around the clock against stablecoins like USDC. Under the SEC’s Innovation Exemption, the venue can list these tokens without the issuing company’s consent, unless the company objects within 30 days.

#5192. Consensys launches cross-ledger settlement and routing stack for institutional networks

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The Sovereign Network Interoperability stack supports securities settlement, cross-currency payments, and collateral mobility while allowing institutions to retain control of their ledgers.

Consensys on Monday announced a new technology stack designed to let banks and financial institutions settle transactions across independently operated blockchains without moving assets off their native ledgers.

Dubbed "Sovereign Network Interoperability," the product was launched at Devcon in Mumbai. It supports collateral mobility, securities settlement, wholesale and cross-currency payments, as well as  funds and asset servicing, according to a statement.

Consensys said the software stack includes a Cross Ledger Interoperability Protocol, built on a decade of research into zero-knowledge proofs. This architecture ensures bridgeless interoperability, meaning assets stay on their native ledgers while cross-ledger state and execution are verified cryptographically, it added.

Under the system, an institution specifies its transaction requirements and approved counterparties. The software selects an eligible route, coordinates execution on the participating ledgers and tracks the outcome.

"We are bringing deep protocol expertise, transaction coordination and complete support into one product," Consensys President David Cunningham said in the statement. "The result is a simpler path to each new connection, with verifiable outcomes and clear accountability for keeping the full stack working for years to come."

The product will also support the Ethereum Economic Zone standard, the Inter-Blockchain Communication protocol, and other interoperability technologies, per the statement. EVM-based networks are available now, while Canton connectivity is planned.

The launch follows Consensys' Oct. 8 partnership with ClearToken to develop infrastructure for round-the-clock settlement of tokenized securities and cash. In September, the company also announced plans to separate its consumer and institutional businesses, with MetaMask becoming an independent consumer-focused company and Consensys retaining its protocols and institutional infrastructure operations.

#5190. Bitcoin Price Trades Near $77,500 as Midterm Voting Begins

Bitcoin price trades near $77,500 as midterm voting begins, pressured by a record $6.3 billion Binance hack, ETF outflows, a Fed leaning toward a December hike and oil above $100 on Iran tensions.

Bitcoin price traded near $77,500 on Tuesday morning as polls opened for the U.S. midterm elections, down about .6% on the day and roughly $30,000 below its price one year ago, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data.

The vote decides control of Congress and, with it, the fate of the CLARITY Act, the market structure bill that passed the House 294-134 in July 2025 and failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15.

Prediction markets on Kalshi give Democrats a 94% chance of taking the House and 74% for the Senate. A Democratic Senate would likely put Sen. Elizabeth Warren in line to chair the Banking Committee. If Congress does not act before the new session convenes on Jan. 3, the bill dies and must be reintroduced.

Crypto groups spent more than $200 million on the midterms, CNBC reported. Analysts at TD Cowen see a Democratic sweep as the most probable outcome and the least favorable one for the industry; a divided Congress, in their view, leaves room for a narrower deal.

The bitcoin price action into the vote has been one-directional. Bitcoin price opened November near $78,500 and has lost about $2,000 since. October closed down about 7%, the second straight October decline.

On Oct. 28, after a hawkish Federal Reserve hold and weak earnings from Alphabet and Samsung, the price fell below $80,000 for the first time in weeks, with about $356 million in leveraged positions liquidated.

The larger shock came the next day. On Oct. 29, attackers the FBI attributes to North Korea drained about $6.3 billion from Binance’s hot wallets, the largest crypto theft on record.

Bitcoin price held strong but fell about 4% on Friday and more than $1.3 billion in leveraged positions were liquidated. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $643.3 million that day, their largest single-day outflow since June 25, according to SoSoValue data. Another $399 million left on Monday.

Bitcoin recovered to $78,491 late Sunday before sliding again on Monday and Tuesday. Traders are watching support between $76,264 and $76,476, which lines up with the 200-day exponential moving average. Below that sits the September low of $74,967.

The 50-day EMA near $80,800 is the first resistance above.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index read 37 on Monday, in “fear” territory.

The macro backdrop offers little support. The Fed held rates at 3.75% to 4.00% on Oct. 28 by a 10-2 vote, with two officials dissenting in favor of a hike, after raising rates in September for the first time since July 2023.

Polymarket puts the odds of a December hike near 71%. September PCE inflation ran at 3.6% year over year and third-quarter GDP grew at a 3.4% annualized pace.

The 10-year Treasury yield touched 5.36% in early October, its highest since 2002, before easing to about 4.91% on Monday.

Energy sits at the center of the inflation picture. Brent crude fell 7.7% on Monday to $104.66 a barrel after Iran and the U.S. signaled openness to talks, its biggest one-day drop since July, CNBC reported.

The move reversed in part on Tuesday, with Brent back near $106.60 after reports of a U.S. strike on Iran’s Qeshm Island. Disruption around the Strait of Hormuz has kept crude above $100 and headline inflation elevated, which keeps the Fed on a tightening path.

Equities have added to the pressure. The Nasdaq fell 2.9% on Friday, its worst day since June, after results from Intel and Amazon raised questions about AI capital spending, and lost 5.3% in October. Bitcoin price has moved with technology stocks through the selloff rather than against them.

The calendar is full. The October jobs report lands Friday, with consensus near 65,000 after September’s 29,000. October CPI follows on Nov. 10 and the Fed’s next decision on Dec. 9. Binance’s next proof-of-reserves snapshot is due Wednesday.

Options markets are calm, with Deribit’s DVOL index near 36.5, a sign traders expect no large swings. Seasonality cuts the other way: Bitcoin price fell 36.6% in November 2018 and 16.2% in November 2022, the two most recent midterm years, both during bear markets.

#5189. NEAR governance approves proposal to cut token issuance ceiling to 1.6%

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

NEAR's House of Stake passed HSP-029, which lowers the network's maximum annual issuance rate from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months.

NEAR Protocol's House of Stake governance body has approved a proposal to lower the network's maximum annual issuance rate from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months.

HSP-029, which passed the ballot on Monday with overwhelming support, retains the existing 90/10 split of newly issued tokens between staking participants and the protocol treasury, and changes only that single protocol parameter. This would not alter the emissions mechanism.

Once implemented, the issuance ceiling declines each epoch linearly at roughly 3.75 basis points per month, reaching the 1.6% target at the 24th month. This is essentially to slow dilution and reduce long-term sell pressure of the NEAR token NEAR Protocol (NEAR), as the network said its bootstrapping phase is largely over.

"This is how NEAR's economics evolve transparently and programmatically: token holders put a core parameter to a public vote, and the new rate rolls out epoch by epoch on a published schedule," NEAR said in an X post. "For NEAR's tokenomics, that means lower long-term issuance and a supply schedule anyone can verify."

The proposal estimates the ramp would avoid roughly 11.8 million NEAR of issuance over the first two years and 66.1 million over six years, leaving the supply about 4.36% smaller than under the existing 2.5% ceiling. Daily issuance would fall from approximately 89,500 NEAR to 57,300 NEAR at full implementation, while gross staking yield is projected to decline from 5.268% to 3.372% over the same period, assuming the staking participation rate measured in September remains unchanged.

For the proposal to go into full effect, the House of Stake Security Council will need to review the result before ratification. A 90-day grace period follows, after which validators must adopt a protocol upgrade for the change to become binding. NEAR previously cut the maximum issuance from 5% to 2.5% in October 2025.

#5187. Claude Fable 5.5, GPT 6.2 Astra Could Be Right Around the Corner, Leaks Suggest

Claude Fable 5.5, GPT 6.2 Astra Could Be Right Around the Corner, Leaks Suggest

Users on X say Anthropic is quietly testing an unreleased version of Mythos and Fable, and OpenAI is about to launch GPT 6.2 Astra.

Nobody has confirmed anything but the rumors are based on a handful of showcase demos, plus a catalog entry and a hidden website label that were both removed soon after appearing.

Fable is the top commercial tier of Claude, the chatbot that competes with ChatGPT, and the priciest flagship Anthropic sells for writing software and handling office work. Mythos is a restricted model that Anthropic offers to a select group of clients, including government and cyber giants due to its capabilities.

Anthropic filed for an IPO, its first sale of shares to the public, on October 26, reporting $16.29 billion in revenue for the first half of 2026 against $1.16 billion a year earlier.

Anthropic launched Fable 5 on June 9, pulled it offline for 18 days under U.S. export controls, which are government limits on who may use a technology, and released Fable 5.1 on September 1. Opus 5.5 followed on September 22, and Anthropic says it performs at the level of Fable 5.1 on most tasks at a fraction of the price.

The chatter began on October 1, when users posted screenshots of Claude still labeled Fable 5.1 beside outputs they said looked stronger. On October 11, pseudonymous user Chetaslua, known for sharing insights on unreleased models and spotting changes in outputs, logs and other signals of new models before they are officially announced, wrote that “Claude Opus 5.5 is routing to a new model,” adding that it could be either Fable 5.5 or a new Opus model.

🚨 Claude Opus 5.5 is routing to a new model < this can be fable 5.5 or new opus i have no idea >

Drew every frame of this animation using JS. The life of a Human

Two Prompts : literally 4 lines Time : 2 HoursTokens : 27 Miliion Thinking Effort : Max pic.twitter.com/6Uy9qxQV7e

— Chetaslua (@chetaslua) October 11, 2026

Routing means a request is quietly sent to a different model behind the scenes. Some users suspect an A/B test, in which Anthropic shows a new version to a slice of users to compare it with the current one while the screen keeps the old name.

“Is this Fable 5.5? Saw posts that Opus is routed to Fable 5.5, so I decided to make a complex 2D animation with it,” AI developer Shikhar—known for vibe coding a Spiderman game that went viral on social media—wrote on X, next to an animation made with Opus, but with a superior quality compared to what the model usually outputs.

Min Choi, another reputable leaker in the AI community, listed the clip in a roundup of examples: “A 30-second Spider-Verse-style 2D animation: a character on a city street, then a web-swing over the rooftops.” Shikhar posed the model as a question rather than a finding, but the timing helped feed the FOMO.

10. A 30-second Spider-Verse-style 2D animation: a character on a city street, then a web-swing over the rooftops.

He says it's one HTML file (WebGL, GLSL shaders and 2D Canvas), made with Opus while it was reportedly routing to Fable 5.5. He asks "Is this Fable 5.5?" himself.…

On October 13, an automated update to LiteLLM, an open-source tool developers use to connect to many AI providers, added an entry named “claude-fable-5-5” after Microsoft’s Azure cloud catalog listed it as generally available. The entry copied its price and limits from Fable 5.1, its notes say no source states a price, capability or text-length limit for Fable 5.5, and it was later removed.

“Mythos 5.5 is already listed HTML source code of Anthropic’s official website,” Kim Isenberg—who also goes by the pseudonymous "Chubby," and is one of the most active and followed accounts in the AI community—wrote on Sunday alongside a screenshot of the code behind Anthropic’s Mythos page. “Thanks to the anonymous person who sent me the tip via email,” the account posted.

Mythos is the restricted twin of Fable, the same underlying model with lighter safety filters, reserved for vetted cybersecurity and biology researchers.

Thanks to the anonymous person who sent me the tip via email 🙂 pic.twitter.com/HqCPUD3inP

Chetaslua counted six appearances of the label in the page’s hidden code, in an invisible headline and in the alt text of the logo, the description attached to an image for screen readers. A check of the live page on Monday showed only Mythos 5.1, the version Anthropic released alongside Fable 5.1 in September.

OpenAI also has its own share of AI FOMO.

“OpenAI is quietly A/B testing a new variant called GPT-6.2 Astra, and it’s already showing up in Codex usage analytics,” a user known as TokenGremlin wrote on Sunday. “The model picker still says GPT-6 Astra, but the usage breakdown for some accounts now lists gpt-6.2-astra separately.”

Codex is OpenAI’s coding assistant, and a screenshot circulating on X showed gpt-6.2-astra at 19.8% of one account’s usage, next to gpt-6-astra at 80%. OpenAI has not announced a 6.2 model, and it released GPT-6 Astra on September 3.

“GPT-6.2 Astra is already showing up in codex usage analytics, as per Token Gremlin,” Chubby wrote, predicting a release “very soon, in response to Fable 5.5 and Anthropic’s IPO.”

“Next week is going to be exciting,” Chubby wrote.

Anthropic’s S-1, the registration document a company files with U.S. regulators before selling shares, lists the Nasdaq ticker ANT and names Goldman Sachs and Morgan Stanley first among its underwriters, the banks that sell the shares to investors. The share count and price range are still blank.

Because Anthropic first filed confidentially in June, SEC rules require the paperwork to be public for 15 days before the roadshow, the marketing tour where banks pitch the shares to investors, which puts the earliest start at November 10.

#5186. BlackRock’s IBIT sees biggest Bitcoin ETF outflows since June

BlackRock’s Bitcoin ETF shed $405.3 million on Monday as US spot Bitcoin funds opened November with nearly $399 million in net outflows.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) recorded its largest daily outflow since June on Monday as US-listed spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) kicked off November with further withdrawals.

The funds recorded $398.6 million in net outflows, led by $405.3 million from IBIT, according to Farside Investors data.

IBIT’s outflow was its largest since June 26, when investors pulled $444.5 million. Fidelity’s Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) attracted $6.7 million on Monday, partially offsetting the outflows.

The withdrawals followed $515 million in net outflows from US spot Bitcoin ETFs in October. The funds have recorded around $38 million in net outflows so far in 2026.

“I wouldn’t interpret these outflows as a rejection of digital assets,” Edwin Mata, CEO of tokenization company Brickken, said in commentary published Monday. “Investors reduce exposure when uncertainty rises, particularly after an event that raises questions about the security of market infrastructure.”

The withdrawals came amid broader crypto market volatility following the $6.3 billion Binance hack.

Bitcoin briefly fell below $76,400 on Monday, its lowest level since August, according to CoinGecko. The cryptocurrency traded at around $76,600 at publication, down 3.7% over the past seven days.

US spot Ether (ETH) ETFs also recorded about $140 million in net outflows on Monday, extending their losing streak to six trading days after posting a record $1.61 billion in monthly outflows in October, according to SoSoValue data.

#5184. New Jersey Drops Coinbase Staking Order After Three Years

New Jersey’s securities regulator has rescinded its 2023 cease-and-desist order and $5 million penalty against Coinbase’s staking service.

New Jersey’s securities regulator has withdrawn the 2023 cease-and-desist order that barred Coinbase from offering staking to new customers in the state, ending a three-year standoff that cost residents an estimated $15 million in forgone rewards.

The Bureau of Securities notice, dated Oct. 30 and signed by Acting Bureau Chief Keith A. Alt, rescinds the order and the $5 million penalty that came with it. The withdrawal is without prejudice and without a ruling on the merits.

Coinbase disclosed the development in its third-quarter report filed with the Securities and Exchange Commission, which states that the regulator informed the company of its intent to rescind on Oct. 29.

Coinbase CEO Brian Armstrong said New Jersey users would regain access to staking “very soon.”

As of Monday, the exchange’s eligibility page continued to list New Jersey among the states where customers cannot stake new principal, and no reopening date has been announced.

The order traces to June 6, 2023, when the New Jersey bureau joined securities regulators in nine other states — Alabama, California, Illinois, Kentucky, Maryland, South Carolina, Vermont, Washington and Wisconsin — in a coordinated action against Coinbase’s staking program.

The states alleged the service amounted to an unregistered securities offering under state law.

California led the task force; its action covered more than 600,000 accounts. In July 2023, Coinbase agreed with California, New Jersey, South Carolina and Wisconsin to block customers in those states from staking new funds pending final adjudication. Maryland reached a similar agreement in October 2023.

Customers who had staked before the orders kept earning rewards, but could not add principal or compound the rewards they received.

The state cases ran parallel to the SEC’s June 2023 lawsuit against Coinbase, which named staking among its claims. The SEC dismissed that case in February 2025. Alabama, Kentucky, Illinois, South Carolina and Vermont dismissed, vacated or withdrew their own actions in March and April 2025. New Jersey is the sixth of the original ten states to step back.

Rescinding the order ends the restriction on new staking in New Jersey. It does not resolve the underlying legal question. The bureau made no finding on whether Coinbase’s staking program is a security, and the “without prejudice” language preserves its ability to file a new action. Coinbase’s own filing calls the state matters “preliminary” and says an adverse resolution “could have a material impact” on the business.

The filing describes a planned rescission rather than a completed one, which explains the gap between Armstrong’s statement and the unchanged eligibility page. The change for New Jersey residents arrives when Coinbase updates its systems, not when the notice was signed.

Coinbase has framed the state restrictions as a competitive disadvantage. In an April 2025 post, the company’s Ryan VanGrack argued the orders singled out one provider while residents could stake through other platforms. Coinbase put forgone rewards across the four restricted states at more than $130 million as of October 2025.

Four states remain. California, Maryland and Wisconsin continue to bar new staking on Coinbase while their cases proceed; Washington permits staking but is pursuing a $4 million fine. Coinbase estimates California residents have missed more than $89 million in rewards since 2023.

The decision lands as federal policy has shifted but not settled. The SEC has withdrawn from the registration-based cases it brought in 2023, and the CLARITY market structure bill passed the House in 2025 but stalled in the Senate. State securities regulators are not bound by the SEC’s enforcement priorities, which is why the staking cases outlived the federal one by more than a year and a half.

A future SEC, or a future bureau chief in Trenton, could revisit the question.

#5183. Liquid Network restores full bitcoin backing following $320 million exploit

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Samson Mow, a Liquid Federation board member, said Blockstream provided the missing bitcoin after the September exploit left nearly 600 BTC unreturned.

The Liquid Federation announced Tuesday that the sidechain's bitcoin reserves are back to full 1:1 backing for all LBTC, nearly two months after an exploit in which approximately $320 million in bitcoin was withdrawn from the network.

Users have been able to swap Bitcoin (BTC) and LBTC again for several weeks through participating providers. Direct Federation peg-outs were previously restricted pending additional security checks, although some semi-automated peg-outs have since resumed. Tuesday's announcement did not say whether the remaining restrictions had been lifted. Reserve balances are published at liquid.network, where Liquid node operators can also verify the backing themselves.

"We extend our sincere appreciation to the Bitcoin community, Liquid users, and ecosystem partners for their patience, understanding, and continued engagement throughout the recovery process," the Liquid Federation said. "Your support and involvement has been instrumental as we worked to restore the network's reserves and strengthen its underlying infrastructure."

About 4,000 BTC was taken from Liquid's Federation wallet in the Sept. 6 exploit. The attacker sent back roughly 3,400 BTC the next day after Blockstream, the developer of Liquid Network's underlying technology, patched the affected bridge nodes, but withheld about 600 BTC. Adam Back-led Blockstream later rejected the attacker's demand for a 10% bug bounty in exchange for returning the remaining coins.

"We will not be a party to the precedent that open-source software developed for the good of the Bitcoin community should subject its developers to paying a ransom that far exceeds their economic participation," Blockstream said at the time.

Liquid Federation board member and former Blockstream executive Samson Mow reported a 50 BTC deposit into the Federation wallet on Oct. 28, leaving 550 BTC outstanding. Another 300 BTC arrived on Monday, followed by a further 250 BTC later that day, bringing the reserves back to full backing. Mow identified Blockstream as the source of the funds. "As stated many times, Blockstream," he wrote on X when asked where the bitcoin was coming from.

Liquid's September incident report traced the exploit to a bug involving cached range proof verifications in Elements, the underlying software used to run the Liquid Network. The attacker was able to create approximately 4,000 unbacked LBTC, which were then used to withdraw bitcoin through Liquid Network wallet and trading platform SideSwap, a Federation member with a peg-out authorization key. Liquid said its network operators were not hacked and no keys were compromised.

Elements v23.3.4 was released on Sept. 9 to address the vulnerability. Liquid resumed producing blocks later that day, with user transactions returning on Sept. 10, although Federation peg-outs remained disabled at that stage.

In its latest update, the Federation said it was preparing changes to governance and oversight, as well as additional technical and operational safeguards. It expects to share further details in the coming weeks.

#5182. Kraken restricts accounts linked to suspected $133M EU crypto theft

Crypto.com also took steps to block funds tied to the suspected theft, while Kraken said its systems and customer accounts were not compromised.

Update (Nov. 4, 2026, 8:20 am UTC): This article has been updated to include comments from Kyrrex.

Update (Nov. 3, 2026, 8:08 pm UTC): The headline has been clarified to specify that Kraken’s account restrictions relate to accounts linked to the suspected theft. Kraken says its systems and customer accounts were not compromised.Update (Nov. 3, 2026, 11:51 am UTC): This article has been updated with a revised headline and responses from Kraken, OKX and Crypto.com.

Kraken has restricted accounts linked to a suspected crypto theft of more than $133 million from an unidentified European Union entity and is working with law enforcement to support recovery efforts.

“This incident did not originate on the Kraken platform, and we’re actively investigating reports that some of the assets were subsequently moved through accounts on our platform,” Kraken told Cointelegraph. “We’ve restricted the accounts in question and are working with law enforcement to support recovery efforts.”

Kraken’s statement follows blockchain investigator ZachXBT’s disclosure on Tuesday that he identified another $27 million in withdrawals from centralized exchanges, increasing the suspected losses from the $106 million he reported on Monday. He said some withdrawals occurred after his initial disclosure.

Bitquery reported that the suspected stolen funds were withdrawn from accounts at Kraken, Crypto.com and OKX. It also found that $62.98 million in stablecoins was converted into 27,553 Ether (ETH) within roughly four hours.

Kraken said its security systems had not been compromised, no customer accounts had been taken over and there was no ongoing risk to its customers.

Crypto.com told Cointelegraph that it had taken steps to prevent funds associated with the incident from moving through its platform.

“We are aware of this report and have taken appropriate action to block any funds associated with this third-party incident from moving through Crypto.com,” a spokesperson said. “Our platform remains fully operational, and all user funds are secure and accessible.”

OKX acknowledged Cointelegraph’s request for comment and said it was looking into the matter.

As of 13:47 UTC Monday, approximately $78.3 million remained in wallets linked to the suspected attacker, while $30.5 million had moved out of the tracked addresses, according to Bitquery. Only $3.74 million remained in USDC that issuer Circle could potentially freeze, while Tether had already frozen $1.1 million in USDT.

Bitquery also traced connected transactions to an account at Kyrrex, citing labels from blockchain analytics firm TRM Labs.

Kyrrex told Cointelegraph that it was aware of reports concerning the alleged movement of stolen funds and was closely monitoring the situation.

“While we cannot comment on individual transactions, specific wallet addresses or any related internal reviews, Kyrrex stands ready to cooperate with competent law enforcement and regulatory authorities,” the company said.

“My working theory is a large financial institution in Western Europe was exploited and it’s currently being covered up,” ZachXBT said on his Telegram channel.

He also said the lack of a proper incident disclosure allowed more than $30 million to be laundered, claiming EU law enforcement could otherwise have frozen the funds. The victim’s identity and allegations of a cover-up remain unverified.

ZachXBT said earlier that the attacker was swapping funds into ETH and Monero (XMR) through instant exchanges and cross-chain bridges. He said the laundering patterns did not match those typically used by North Korea’s Lazarus Group.

#5181. North Korea Denies $6.3B Binance Hack, Calls FBI Finding a 'Political Provocation'

North Korea Denies $6.3B Binance Hack, Calls FBI Finding a 'Political Provocation'

North Korea has rejected U.S. findings that it was behind the record hack of Binance, calling the FBI's attribution a "grave political provocation," Yonhap reported on Tuesday.

"It is a grave political provocation against the DPRK that the U.S. misleads the international public opinion as if all the cyber crimes in different parts of the world are our deeds," Kim Yo Jong, the sister of leader Kim Jong Un, said in a statement carried by the state-run Korean Central News Agency. She called the accusation a "sinister" attempt to damage North Korea's international image.

Kim also turned the blame back on Washington, pointing to the Trump administration's plan to use confiscated crypto as a strategic reserve. Anyone who looks at who will end up with "the so-called stolen cryptocurrency" once it is retrieved "may get a clue about who is behind the hacking incident," she said.

The FBI said on Saturday that North Korean hackers it calls TraderTraitor were responsible for the theft of more than $6 billion from Binance, confirming the attribution blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis made last week. The FBI attributed the $1.5 billion hack of crypto exchange Bybit in February 2025 to the same group. North Korea has denied previous accusations of hacking.

The hackers are moving their haul quickly. North Korea is laundering the funds at a rate of roughly one new wallet every 25 seconds, across about 12,000 wallets, Decrypt reported on Monday. About $2.85 billion of the $6.43 billion stolen has been frozen or seized, compared with roughly 3% of the funds taken from Bybit.

Not all of the stolen funds are moving at the same pace. As of Monday, more than $1 billion in ETH and about $1.42 billion in BTC had seen limited onward movement, while other funds were moving rapidly across chains, blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis said. The activity so far is broadly consistent with the "immediate layering" phase seen after past North Korean hacks, though funds repeatedly returning to earlier addresses across thousands of transactions suggests automation, the firm said. It expects the scale of the theft to "strain the actors' trusted laundering channels," pushing funds toward bridges, instant exchanges and swapping services.

As of Nov 2, over $1B in ETH and ~$1.42 billion in BTC stolen from Binance have seen limited onward movement since the theft.

Other stolen funds are moving rapidly across chains.

These funds are not all progressing through the laundering process at the same pace. pic.twitter.com/ZEVCZzCDRM

— Chainalysis (@chainalysis) November 2, 2026

Binance did not respond to the BBC's questions about North Korea's alleged involvement. A company spokesperson told the outlet that a net $4.72 billion had been withdrawn since withdrawals resumed on Sunday, a "relatively limited portion" of total assets of about $157 billion.

By 10 p.m. ET on Monday, hourly deposits were exceeding withdrawals, "reflecting improving user activity and stabilizing flows," Binance tweeted. The exchange has since restored withdrawals for dozens more tokens on Ethereum and Solana.

Binance also said on Monday that users holding its 10 largest tokens, including BTC, ETH, USDT and USDC, can now verify that their balances are fully backed, using a zero-knowledge proof of its October 31 reserves snapshot. It has covered about 95% of the stolen assets, with the rest made up of less liquid tokens that take longer to buy, and plans to take a wider snapshot on Wednesday.

#5178. Ethereum Layer 2 Aztec gets Tierra V6 upgrade to support faster private apps

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Tierra V6 fixes a critical V5 security issue and speeds up private transaction proofs, with new apps launching or preparing to launch on Aztec.

The upgrade resolves vulnerabilities found in earlier versions, including a critical issue in V5, the foundation said. It also adds a new AI-based system for security testing.

When asked why the critical issue wasn't fixed sooner, an Aztec spokesperson said the fix required the whole network to move to a new version.

"Because Aztec is a decentralized network, if a critical vulnerability is discovered, the entire network must upgrade to the new version to implement the fix," the spokesperson told The Block.

Aztec has no central operator that can make the change on its own, the spokesperson said, adding that there are plans to make future upgrades faster if vulnerabilities are discovered.

Aztec said its network has more than 3,000 validators, no centralized sequencer, and no privileged viewing keys.

Besides the security fixes, Tierra makes private transaction proofs twice as fast, taking one second to generate on a native device, according to the foundation.

Messages from Ethereum can now reach Aztec in as little as 12 seconds, compared with an average of two minutes before, the foundation said, noting that the change speeds up deposits and other transactions between the networks.

Developers on Aztec can choose which functions of their apps are private and which are public, the foundation said, adding that private functions run and generate proofs on the user's device, while public functions run on the network.

The upgrade comes shortly after Aztec Labs relaunched zk.money, a self-custodial wallet for private crypto payments, three years after discontinuing the original product.

"With Aztec Tierra and zk.money, users can deposit directly from any Ethereum wallet and make lightning-fast private transactions without complicated bridging workflows," Joe Andrews, co-founder and CEO of Aztec Labs, said in a statement.

Other apps launching or preparing to launch include Galactica for private AI inference, Nemi for private perpetuals trading, and the private browser wallet Nulo, according to the announcement.

Xochi supports private intents and trading, while Nyx lets users make private payments to any Ethereum wallet. Olla offers liquid staking for the Aztec token.

Rolling Chat, a private message board based on Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin's Aztec experiments, is also on the list.

Aztec also introduced a new brand and website alongside Tierra, describing its aim as becoming the platform for "unstoppable private apps."

#5177. CFTC Opens U.S. Stock Market to Equity Perpetual Futures

The CFTC has cleared perpetual futures on individual stocks to trade on dual-registered U.S. exchanges for the first time.

Perpetual futures on individual stocks can begin trading on U.S.-regulated exchanges for the first time, the Commodity Futures Trading Commission said Tuesday, extending a product that was confined to offshore crypto venues a year ago into the equity market.

In a statement, CFTC Chairman Michael S. Selig said the contracts, which he called “Equity Perps,” were deemed approved under Commission Regulation 40.3 as security futures products.

They may list on exchanges that hold a CFTC designated contract market registration and a notice registration with the Securities and Exchange Commission as a national securities exchange for security futures.

“This expansion marks a significant step towards allowing U.S. market participants, consistent with applicable law, to avail themselves of the advantages of perpetual futures,” Selig said. He said bringing the products inside the agency’s framework means Americans no longer need to “turn to offshore, and often unregulated, venues.”

The contracts fall under the Commodity Exchange Act, federal securities law and the rulebooks of both agencies. The statement names no exchange, lists no contracts and sets no launch date.

Those details are expected to come from the designated contract markets themselves.

Perpetual futures have no expiration date. Instead of settling on a fixed day, longs and shorts exchange periodic funding payments that keep the contract price tied to spot. The structure has dominated crypto derivatives for years, with most volume on platforms outside U.S. jurisdiction.

The CFTC approved the first U.S.-listed bitcoin perpetual, Kalshi’s BTCPERP, on May 29, and Kalshi went live with the product soon after. The same day, staff granted no-action relief that lets Coinbase Financial Markets route U.S. clients into offshore perp and options liquidity. Kalshi added gold and silver perpetuals in September.

In his statement Tuesday, Selig described the equity contracts as an extension of that May decision “beyond digital commodities and into traditional securities.”

The legal basis is older. The Commodity Futures Modernization Act of 2000 created a joint CFTC-SEC regime for security futures products, and the two agencies signed a memorandum of understanding on March 11 to coordinate rulemaking and enforcement.

Selig cited both, and repeated SEC Chairman Paul S. Atkins’s description of security futures as stranded in a regulatory “no man’s land” for lack of harmonization. The agencies had set out a joint crypto asset taxonomy earlier in the year.

Not everyone accepts the CFTC’s reading. CME Grou  sued the agency in June over the Kalshi approval, arguing that a contract built on exchanged funding payments is a swap under Dodd-Frank, not a future.

That case is pending. An equity version adds the SEC’s jurisdiction to the mix and may draw a new set of objections from securities-side participants.

The approval lands five days after the largest theft in crypto’s history. On Oct. 29, attackers that TRM Labs and Chainalysis link to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group drained about $6.3 billion from Binance’s hot wallets in roughly 20 minutes.

Binance reopened withdrawals on Sunday and says it has replenished about 95% of affected assets.

Selig used the breach to press the case he made in Tuesday’s statement.

“The real issue here is that these are unregulated offshore exchanges, and they don’t have the same sorts of safeguards that we have in our financial markets here in the United States,” he told CNBC on Oct. 30.

He said the hack “proves our exact point” about the agency’s October proposal, an advance notice of rulemaking that would create a voluntary federal registration path for crypto exchanges with cybersecurity, proof-of-reserves and custody requirements. Selig has said the CFTC cannot make that registration mandatory without Congress.

The equity perp approval works from the same premise. Perpetuals built the offshore derivatives business; moving them onto dual-registered U.S. exchanges puts the margin, clearing and surveillance rules of two federal agencies around them.

Selig framed it as a choice between regulated access and “being forced to turn to offshore, and often unregulated, venues.”

#5173. Liquid Network Restores Full Bitcoin Backing Two Months After $320M Exploit

Liquid Network Restores Full Bitcoin Backing Two Months After $320M Exploit

The Liquid Network's Bitcoin reserves have been fully restored, two months after self-described white-hat hackers drained most of them, the Liquid Federation said on Monday.

The federation said it had reestablished 1:1 Bitcoin backing for all LBTC, the token that represents Bitcoin on Liquid. The milestone follows the restoration of swaps between BTC and LBTC at a 1:1 rate through participating providers several weeks ago, it said.

The Liquid Federation is pleased to announce that the network’s bitcoin reserves have been fully restored, reestablishing 1:1 BTC backing for all LBTC.

This follows the restoration of BTC<>LBTC swaps several weeks ago, which have since allowed…

— Liquid Network 🌊 (@Liquid_BTC) November 3, 2026

Blockstream CEO Adam Back, whose company develops the technology behind Liquid, posted a screenshot of the network's dashboard showing assets matching liabilities at 100%, with a reserve deficit of 0.00 BTC. Users running their own Liquid nodes can verify the federation's reserves directly, the federation said.

It did not say how the shortfall left by the hack was covered.

Launched by Blockstream in 2018, Liquid is a Bitcoin sidechain, a separate blockchain that runs alongside Bitcoin, designed for faster and more private transfers. Users lock up BTC in a wallet controlled by the federation, a group of exchanges and other crypto companies that run the network, and receive the same amount of LBTC in return.

Asked on X what would stop a repeat of the hack, Back listed three hardened "lines of defense": refreshed peg-out authorization keys now held in cold hardware wallets, disabled proof validation caches along with a bug fix for nodes, and consensus rules that have been hardened to "fail closed." The network has also been through multiple third-party audits, he said.

"And lesson: treat with suspicion (don't trust, verify) AI suggested fixes," Back wrote.

Back had pointed to AI's growing role in security in an interview with Bloomberg Television on October 20, describing "a wave of AI-assisted security analysis" over the previous couple of months. "AI, of course, is a tool to help develop code and to secure the code, but also it can be used to find defects," he said, adding that it had been "affecting many companies in the sector." He said then that the hacked funds remained "under investigation."

The federation said it is modernizing Liquid's governance structure, introducing clearer responsibilities and oversight, and adding further technical and operational safeguards, with more details to come in the coming weeks.

On September 6, purported white-hat hackers withdrew about 4,000 BTC, then worth around $320 million, from Liquid's federation wallet, communicating with Blockstream through PGP-signed messages embedded in Bitcoin transactions.

The next day, they returned 3,400 BTC, worth about $269 million, after Blockstream said Liquid's bridge nodes had been patched, but kept roughly 598 BTC. Ledger CTO Charles Guillemet said that if the remaining coins were a negotiated reward, the arrangement looked "more like extortion than white-hat hacking."

Blockstream later refused to pay a ransom for the outstanding Bitcoin. "Taking assets without authorization and withholding their return is a crime, not responsible disclosure," the company said. "It is not white-hat activity. It is theft."

#5172. Binance says hackers used fake job interview, internal AI tool in $6.3 billion breach

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Binance said it detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, including similar attempts involving fraudulent interviews and malicious software.

Binance said hackers used a fraudulent job interview to compromise an employee’s computer, then used an internal AI tool to access its wallet infrastructure and steal more than $6.3 billion in digital assets.

In an initial incident report published Tuesday, the world's largest crypto exchange detailed how attackers used malware and stolen credentials to access its wallet-signing systems. Binance said it has frozen or intercepted about $2.85 billion in assets and replenished 98% of impacted assets.

"This wasn't one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign. We're publishing the full report because the lessons go far beyond Binance," co-CEO Richard Teng said on X. "The speed of coordination could not be matched in traditional finance."

Binance said the FBI had linked the attack to North Korea, while its own investigation found that hackers used social engineering, malware, and compromised internal AI tooling to gain access to its wallet-signing infrastructure and authorize unauthorized transactions.

According to the report, the attackers first targeted a Wallet DevOps engineer in June through a tampered Homebrew installation downloaded while he was trying to install an AI coding tool. Binance said its security team detected the anomaly, replaced the device, and rotated the affected credentials, but the incident exposed information about the engineer's role and colleagues. In September, attackers then targeted another employee through a fraudulent recruitment process, posing as company executives before tricking him into downloading a malicious coding assignment that infected his work computer and gave hackers unauthorized access.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used the compromised employee's computer and an internal AI tool to gain control of two other engineers' devices, discovering a container deployment permission on one of them. Nine days later, they used the stolen credentials to deploy a container and run a malicious script that launched the wallet attack.

Binance said it detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyberattacks throughout 2026, many involving similar tactics such as fraudulent interviews, malicious developer tools, and social engineering. On Monday, North Korean leader Kim Jong-un's sister called FBI claims linking North Korea to the hack a "grave political provocation."

Binance said it has since closed the entry points used in the attack, rebuilt or replaced affected systems, strengthened device controls, and expanded AI-based behavioral anomaly detection.

Separately, the exchange continued restoring normal operations, reopening withdrawals in phases for dozens of assets across BSC, Ethereum, Solana, XRP, Sui, Arbitrum, Optimism, TRON and Base networks starting at 4 a.m. UTC on Nov. 3. Binance said it was prioritizing tokens based on user demand over the past week. The exchange later reported that hourly deposits exceeded withdrawals, describing the shift as a sign that flows were stabilizing and user activity was improving.

#5170. Bitcoin price threatens $77.7K support loss with recent buyers underwater

Bitcoin fell below the realized price of the one-to-three-month-old buyer cohort in a move that risked flipping it to new resistance.

Bitcoin (BTC) risks turning $77,700 into new resistance as recent buyers fall into an aggregate loss, analysis warns.

Data from onchain analytics platform CryptoQuant shows that BTC/USD is now trading below the realized price, or cost basis, of investors who entered the market between one and three months ago.

It defines this investor cohort as speculative traders who are thus more sensitive to short-term price volatility. Their cost basis historically formed upward-trending support during Bitcoin bull markets.

“When BTC falls below this level, however, the same cost basis can later act as resistance as underwater traders use a return to breakeven as an opportunity to exit,” a CryptoQuant contributor said in Monday’s analysis.

The cost basis, as measured by CryptoQuant’s Trader Onchain Realized Price Bands metric, currently stands at $77,700, while BTC/USD trades at around $76,500.

“What is also notable is that the Trader Realized Price has been rising steadily while BTC has been correcting. The gap between price and the cost basis has now narrowed significantly,” the contributor added.

BTC/USD fell below the Trader Onchain Realized Price after trading above it since Aug. 19. The Price Bands include upper and lower trailing levels around the cost basis set at fixed profit and loss margins. The nearest lower band to the cost basis sits at a 12% aggregate loss, currently equal to $68,400. Price last fell through it at the start of June, subsequently deviating up to 12.6% below the band over the following month.

Bitcoin Trader Onchain Realized Price Bands. Source: CryptoQuant

Below $77,000, Bitcoin is also approaching the realized price of the broader speculative investor base known as short-term holders (STHs), another trend line that functions as support during uptrends, as well as resistance during downtrend reversals.

Related: BTC heads into US midterm showdown: Three things to know in Bitcoin this week

These entities have held their BTC without selling for up to 155 days and have a cost basis of $75,832 as of Tuesday. This corresponds to a reading of 1.012 on the cohort’s Market Value to Realized Value (MVRV) Ratio metric, which divides the market value of STH coins by their realized value, based on the prices at which they last moved onchain.

The metric shows that once price falls below the MVRV breakeven point corresponding to the STH realized price, the next key psychological support level is an MVRV reading of 0.85. This is described as an area of “tactical capitulation” or “short trap.”

The start of June also marked the last time that BTC/USD fell through the 0.85 MVRV level, subsequently recovering it in mid-August.

Bitcoin STH realized price and MVRV data. Source: CryptoQuant

#5168. CFTC approves individual stock perpetual futures trading in US for the first time

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Perpetual futures contracts surged in popularity over the past year, particularly after disruptions in global supply chains in the Middle East earlier this year at the onset of the war.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission approved the ability for exchanges in the United States to offer perpetual futures contracts tied to individual stocks — just months after those assets were put into the spotlight by President Donald Trump.

On Tuesday, CFTC Chair Michael Selig greenlit the ability to trade those perpetual futures contracts for the first time in the U.S. for platforms.

"This expansion marks a significant step towards allowing U.S. market participants, consistent with applicable law, to avail themselves of the advantages of perpetual futures that have led to their emergence as widely sought tools for risk-management and price-discovery," Selig said.

Perpetuals, or perps, are a type of futures contract that don't have an expiration date and allow people to bet on the price movement of assets without owning them directly.

Perpetual futures contracts surged in popularity over the past year, particularly after disruptions in global supply chains in the Middle East earlier this year at the onset of the war, after the U.S. and Israel conducted missile strikes on Iran, which triggered supply shocks. That happened over the weekend, so traditional oil futures markets were closed, but platforms like Hyperliquid operate 24/7, so much of the trading happened onchain.

Over the past year, the CFTC has approved a few perpetual futures contracts. The agency first opened the door for bitcoin perpetual futures contracts in the U.S. when its staff greenlit KalshiEX and Coinbase to list the products. Then in June, the CFTC released a request for comment on crude oil perpetual contracts and 24/7 trading.

Perpetuals futures contracts have also caught Trump's attention. In August, he said he wanted the CFTC to work to bring Hyperliquid into the U.S.

Both the SEC and CFTC would oversee the contracts, Selig said on Tuesday.

"This framework recognized the complementary roles of the CFTC and SEC in overseeing American financial markets by providing a pathway for innovation at the intersection of the U.S.," Selig said.

#5166. Binance Misses Monthly Luna Classic Burn for First Time Since 2023

Binance Misses Monthly Luna Classic Burn for First Time Since 2023

Centralized exchange Binance has burned Luna Classic tokens on the first of every month since 2023, when Do Kwon's Terra Luna empire came crumbling down. At the start of November, however, that burn has not occurred.

Luna Classic (LUNC) is the original native token for the Terra blockchain, which Terraform Labs founder Do Kwon forked and left behind in 2022 in a bid to rescue his collapsing project. Kwon was later convicted of fraud and sentenced to 15 years in prison for his role in Terra’s $40 billion implosion. Yet the decentralized network he left behind has kept running, propped up by a passionate community of supporters who have sunk time, effort and money into keeping it alive.

Binance started the burns after Terra Classic’s community voted to burn a portion of every on-chain LUNC transaction in late 2022, with the exchange pledging to burn half of the trading fees earned from the token. In practice, this has come in the form of a batch of burns happening at the start of every month since March 2023, but, according to StatsBin data, November's burn has yet to come in.

Some Luna Classic investors are concerned about Binance's lack of monthly burn, while others point to last week's record-breaking hack of the exchange as a reason behind the burn's delay.

"It is an important sign of support and a vital part of the overall burn campaign," Dawid Skinder, brand and user experience ecosystem contributor for Terra Classic, told Decrypt. "It might be that it is just an operational issue after the biggest hack ever. Hopefully that is the case, Terra Classic does not need another problem."

Binance was hacked for $6.3 billion on Friday, which the FBI has attributed to TraderTraitor—the North Korean group blamed for the Bybit hack. It has been labelled the biggest crypto hack in history, with estimated losses at four times the previous record of $1.5 billion from the Bybit heist.

The exchange has been reopening withdrawals on the platform in stages, with the latest taking place late on Monday for dozens of Ethereum and Solana tokens. Some investors have questioned whether the LUNC burn is a priority for the exchange as it attempts to recover from the hack.

— Tango and Cash (@ThisDud30190229) November 2, 2026

"Yes, the hack was a problem," prominent Luna Classic community member Pedro, better known as Vegas, told Decrypt. "At the moment, we are helping them to keep LUNC and LUNA safe."

He has long claimed to be in direct contact with Binance regarding LUNC issues, though this has yet to be verified. Vegas claims Binance is working on security maintenance after the hack and is moving LUNC to a more secure wallet before proceeding with the burn.

Binance did not respond to Decrypt's request for comment.

The exchange has paused its LUNC burns once before, withholding contributions at the end of 2022 after the Terra Classic community voted to re-mint burned tokens as a development fund. Binance halved its burn to 50% of trading fees and only resumed in March 2023, once the community had scrapped the re-mints.

Luna Classic has dropped 7.9% in value over the past week to $0.0000417, per Decrypt data, and is down 99.99% from its all-time high of $119.18. Investors believe the Binance burn being paused is just one of many issues the token faces.

Skinder told Decrypt that Luna Classic needs to fix its lack of products, improve its marketing, conduct a proper audit, and protect itself against quantum attacks. Skinder said he believes in a revival but claims that it is difficult with decentralized governance and "amateur validators."

So, why bother? Why not spend time on another chain?

"There are X number of chains in a better state than Terra Classic. None of them (except Bitcoin, probably) have that history and narrative (biggest crypto revival ever). None of them have 20+ stablecoins that are waiting to be re-pegged and restored," Skinder said. "Low chance of success, big upside if done. Great history to be part of."

#5164. Justin Sun seeks sanctions against World Liberty, alleging Trump-linked firm hid UAE ties

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The dispute over World Liberty's citizenship emerged after the company amended an initial court disclosure that had listed only one entity in its ownership chain and Florida as its sole citizenship.

Tron founder Justin Sun said Tuesday he has filed a motion seeking sanctions against World Liberty Financial, alleging the DeFi protocol failed to disclose its United Arab Emirates citizenship until months into their legal dispute.

Sun, who says he invested $45 million in WLFI tokens, sued the Trump-linked crypto venture in April, accusing it of fraud and unlawfully freezing his tokens.

The dispute over World Liberty's citizenship emerged after the company amended an initial court disclosure that had listed only one entity in its ownership chain and Florida as its sole citizenship. In a subsequent filing, World Liberty identified 22 members in its ownership chain and claimed citizenship in Florida, Delaware, Puerto Rico, and the UAE, according to Sun.

Sun alleges the late disclosure was an attempt to challenge the federal court's jurisdiction and push the case out of open court. World Liberty was "proudly" American when selling tokens, he said, but discovered its UAE roots "only when it needed a way out of an American federal courtroom."

Under federal diversity jurisdiction rules, Sun argued, his case could proceed in federal court only if he and World Liberty were citizens of different jurisdictions. Because a company takes on the citizenship of its owners for these purposes, World Liberty's UAE ties could undermine the court's jurisdiction.

Sun said he plans to refile the lawsuit in California state court after the sanctions motion is resolved.

In May, World Liberty Financial countersued Sun, accusing him of running a "meritless PR stunt" against the Trump-family-backed crypto project after his tokens were frozen.

World Liberty debuted in October 2024 amid significant scrutiny because of its association with President Donald Trump and his family. The WLFI token reached an all-time high of around $0.33 and currently trades near $0.051, giving it a fully diluted valuation of approximately $5.1 billion.

#5163. Binance Says $6.34 Billion Hack Began With Fake Job Interview

Binance’s incident report says the $6.34 billion Oct. 29 theft was a five-month campaign that began with a fake job interview and a poisoned developer tool.

The $6.34 billion theft from Binance began with a fake job interview and ran for five months before a single coin moved, the exchange said Tuesday in its first public account of the Oct. 29 attack.

Another initial incident report from Binance, released alongside a media briefing by co-CEO Richard Teng and chief security officer Jimmy Su, describes a campaign that combined a poisoned software download, a staged recruitment process, malware that evaded endpoint detection and an internal AI tool the attackers turned against the company’s own wallet engineers.

Binance said it has frozen or intercepted about $2.85 billion of the stolen assets and replenished 98% of affected tokens from its own funds, with the balance being bought on the open market.

“This wasn’t one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign,” Teng wrote on X. “We’re publishing the full report because the lessons go far beyond Binance.”

The FBI has attributed the attack to North Korea. Binance’s report cites that finding but stops short of its own attribution. Pyongyang rejected the claim Monday, with Kim Yo Jong, sister of leader Kim Jong Un, calling it a “grave political provocation” through state media.

According to Binance, the first foothold for this hack came in June. A wallet DevOps engineer searched for a popular AI coding tool and downloaded a tampered Homebrew installer.

Binance’s security team caught the anomaly, replaced the device and rotated credentials, but the episode exposed the engineer’s role and the names of colleagues on the wallet infrastructure team.

In September, a second engineer was approached on a legitimate job platform by someone posing as a project director. A video interview with a purported HR representative followed, with the interviewer sharing a personal social media account to build trust. A second interview with a supposed CEO ended with an “assignment”: a code repository the engineer was told to install on his work machine.

The repository replaced a legitimate pyenv component with a malicious version and opened a channel to an attacker-controlled server. Binance said the zero-day malware was not flagged by its endpoint tools at the time.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used that machine and an internal AI tool to reach two more wallet engineers, one of whom held a container deployment token. On Oct. 29, between 17:15 and 19:56 UTC, they logged into a third engineer’s computer, deployed a container with the stolen credentials and launched the drain. Binance said it blocked 57 other suspected North Korea-linked attempts this year using similar lures.

The money left fast. Between 20:05 and 20:09 UTC, five hot wallets sent 233.46 million XRP to an attacker address. Wallets on Solana and Optimism followed with about 970,500 SOL and 44.2 million OP. A Binance hot wallet then sent 18,270 BTC.

The attacker used the compromised signing service to invoke Ethereum’s EIP-7702, which lets an account delegate execution rights to a smart contract without exposing its key, and gave an attacker-controlled contract partial control of a Binance hot wallet.

Suspending withdrawals did not stop that last channel. The attackers had submitted 50 signing transactions to establish on-chain delegations that operated outside Binance’s withdrawal infrastructure. Because the wallet used multi-party computation, no single key existed to revoke them, and the team had to build a procedure on the spot. The final unauthorized transfer, 2,961.9 ETH, settled at 23:56 UTC.

Binance cleared the delegation at 00:10 UTC on Oct. 30.

The report acknowledges the gap. “These challenges extended the time required to fully contain the unauthorized asset movements,” it says, adding that the company is reviewing its signing policy coverage and hot wallet controls.

Customers withdrew $10.97 billion in the 46 hours after withdrawals reopened Sunday, with net outflows of $5.28 billion, according todata Teng shared Tuesday. By 03:00 UTC hourly deposits exceeded withdrawals, a shift Teng called a sign of stabilization. Binance reopened withdrawals for dozens more assets across nine networks on Tuesday.

The breach has become a talking point in Washington. CFTC Chairman Michael Selig said the hack “proves our exact point” about the need for a federal registration path for offshore exchanges, and on Tuesday the agency cleared equity perpetual futures for U.S.-regulated venues, extending the onshoring push it began with Kalshi’s bitcoin perp in May.

Binance said it has rebuilt the wallet system and the internal AI tool in new environments, replaced affected devices, revoked the engineers’ credentials and expanded behavioral anomaly detection.

It is commissioning an independent review, raising spending on endpoint and developer-environment security and planning a research fund on AI security with outside firms. Some technical details, the report says, will stay private.

#5160. France postpones 2027 budget vote, crypto exit-tax proposals scrapped

The National Assembly postponed the vote on the budget’s revenue section for the second time, while the Nov. 17 deadline for the first reading examination nears.

The French National Assembly will continue debating the revenue section of the 2027 state budget on Tuesday instead of holding a planned vote.

According to the Assembly’s end-of-session notice following Monday’s sitting, Tuesday’s agenda includes questions to the government and continued debate on the budget’s revenue section with 758 amendments still pending examination.

This marks the vote’s second delay, after the original Oct. 20 vote was postponed with more than 2,000 amendments awaiting debate, according to French newspaper Le Monde.

The National Assembly has until midnight on Nov. 17 to complete its first-reading examination. If the revenue section is rejected, the government’s original budget proposal will move to the Senate without the amendments adopted by MPs.

Related: EU investors rank crypto above retirement plans for lasting wealth creation: OKX survey

The National Assembly rejected two proposals on Friday that would have applied exit taxes to unrealized crypto gains when taxpayers transferred their tax residence abroad.

Lawmakers rejected the first proposal with 27 votes in favor, 55 against and one abstention, according to the Assembly’s official voting record. The proposal sought to include crypto assets in the country’s existing exit tax regime.

A separate proposal was rejected with 31 votes in favor and 60 against. It would have taxed unrealized gains on household crypto holdings worth more than 800,000 euros ($897,000) when taxpayers transferred their tax residence outside the country, provided they had been tax-resident in France for at least six of the preceding 10 years.

Other crypto-related proposals await debate, including a requirement to report self-custodied crypto holdings worth a combined value of at least 100,000 euros ($112,000) to tax authorities, based on their value at year-end.

Another proposal would exempt gains on crypto payments for goods and services from tax if the total value of those payments doesn’t exceed 1,000 euros ($1,120) during the tax year.

Lawmakers approved a proposal on Oct. 16 to tax gains when crypto assets are converted into fiat-pegged stablecoins. The measure has not become law.

On Tuesday, Polymarket users gave Democrats a 76% chance of securing the Senate, and the House a 94% chance.

Ahead of polls closing in the U.S. midterm elections, bettors on prediction market platforms are increasingly pricing in Democrats taking both the House and Senate.

As of early Tuesday afternoon, Polymarket users gave Democrats a 76% chance of securing the Senate, and the House a 94% chance. Similarly, on Kalshi, bettors said Democrats had a 94% chance of controlling the House and a 75% chance for the Senate. All 435 House seats are up for election, as well as 35 of the 100 Senate seats.

Prediction markets surged in popularity during the 2024 presidential election, emerging as an alternative barometer of public opinion and drawing comparisons to national polling. Since then, platforms like Polymarket and Kalshi have generated significant revenue and reached multibillion-dollar valuations.

But interest in election-related trading has cooled considerably since 2024, according to a Bloomberg report published Tuesday. Politics accounted for just 1% of trading volume on both Kalshi and Polymarket in October, down from more than 60% during the 2024 election, Bloomberg reported.

A shift in parties is pivotal since Democrats are likely to then lead key committees, driving the agenda. For crypto, that could mean that Democrats such as Elizabeth Warren and Sherrod Brown could lead panels overseeing the digital asset industry.

Experts who spoke with The Block last month said that would mean more oversight over key issues, including President Donald Trump's crypto wealth through his family's World Liberty Financial, and less of an emphasis on crypto legislation.

As for key crypto races, such as Ohio, where incumbent Jon Husted will face off against crypto critic and former Sen. Brown, bettors on Kalshi say Brown has a 71% chance of beating Husted. For other races, including lawmakers focused on crypto legislation, Sen. Cory Booker, D.-N.J. has a 98% chance on Kalshi.

Morning Minute: Tom Lee Calls the Binance Hack Bullish for Ethereum

🐂 Tom Lee Calls the Binance Hack Bullish for Ethereum

Lee called that “relatively muted crypto market reaction very constructive” and read it as proof the industry can take a hit this size without cracking.

He pointed to four things behind the bullishness:

Each of those is a thing that went right in 2026 and went badly in 2025.

Now we wait to see what the bid looks like without it. And if Tom Lee’s bullish read is the correct one…
