#5124. How to Kill a Tree Stump, According to Experts—And Why It’s Important for Your Yard’s Health

Removing a tree from your property can be daunting, especially considering you can’t just shout “Timber!” and move on: You might need to know how to kill a tree stump as well. Just as an ailing or overgrown tree may put your roof, patio, or parked cars at risk if limbs start dropping, an untended tree stump may well cause underground damage to pipes or the foundation of your home as its roots keep spreading.

So your upcoming arboreal project may start with tree removal, but it’s important for the health of your lawn and your home to learn how to kill a tree stump in the aftermath. Even if you don’t plan to use the space where the tree once stood, according to the arborists we consulted, a tree stump left just sitting around can cause a host of new problems. While there’s more than one way to remove a tree stump, the consensus is universal: To consider tree removal truly complete, you’ll need to kill a tree stump, too.

“Old stumps attract pests, harbor fungi, and become tripping hazards,” says Melissa Rolston, chief strategy officer at Paramount Landscaping. “Some even try to regrow. Removing them protects your soil, prevents infestation, and keeps your yard design intentional.”

Rolston adds that the pest problem an unattended rotting tree stump causes can spread. Insects like ants and termites that might be drawn to a tempting tree stump can multiply and affect an entire property, potentially damaging healthy plant life directly and/or by spreading fungi or pathogens to nearby trees and plants. That “dead” tree stump may well become the launchpad for invaders that are very much alive—and very harmful to your yard.

Now that we’ve established a few of the reasons why you might need to kill a tree stump, read on to learn about a few methods you can use to get rid of a tree stump for good. Note that if the first approach fails to work on a truly tough tree stump, you can then move on to the second or third technique. If time isn’t a factor (as in, you’re not in a tree-stump removal rush to build a new patio or solarium), then the fourth approach is the least invasive in terms of impact on your property’s ecosystem.

Before you decide which tree stump removal method you’re going with to remove the stump in question, you should figure out how big of a job it is—literally. Stumps of different sizes call for different removal approaches, says Sara Gottlieb, founder and CEO of Liminal Gardens.

“Start by measuring the diameter of the flat, cut surface at its widest point, while accounting for any large bulges or irregular shapes,” says Gottlieb. “You’ll also want to consider the ground-level root flare, surface roots, and the height of the stump above the ground. Together, these measurements help give you a better sense of the stump’s overall size. A stump under 12 inches in diameter would generally be considered small, while one measuring more than 30 inches would be considered large. Surface roots and stump height should also factor into your assessment.”

Small stumps can be ground out in a matter of minutes, whereas massive stumps may be too big for practical grinding. Chemical or fire stump removal can cause enough damage to large stumps that they are indeed “killed” and might not need full removal; the roots will stop growing if the stump is effectively killed off. For medium-sized stumps, it’s more a matter of your budget, patience, and DIY know-how when it comes to which method you choose from the options below.

Almost every expert we reached out to for tree-stump removal tips recommended using a chemical herbicide to quickly and effectively vanquish unwanted tree stumps. For many of our pros, it was the top suggestion. Benjamin Hanley of Hanley Landscape Design tells AD that “targeted herbicides like glyphosate (potassium nitrate) or triclopyr can be applied directly to a fresh cut or in drilled holes in the stump to kill the root system.” This can be a highly effective way to completely kill off a stump, but be warned: Those chemicals can be dangerous. It’s crucial to use proper protection—and to keep kids and pets at bay—if you’re using this tree-stump removal method.

· Herbicide (potassium nitrate or triclopyr)

Herbicide has been applied into the drilled holes of this tree stump.

Use a chainsaw to cut the stump down as short as you safely can. Next, drill multiple holes down into the stump, with the goal of sinking them at least eight to 10 inches down.

Generously pour your herbicide of choice down into each of the holes you drilled, taking extra care not to get any of the powder or liquid on exposed skin or anywhere near your face. Then pour about a cup of water into each hole to activate the chemicals.

Cover the treated tree stump with a tarp. Secure the tarp with bungees, ropes, rocks, or a combination of items heavy enough to keep it down tightly. Within a matter of days, that stump should be effectively dead, but the process may stretch out a bit depending on the stump’s resilience. (See below FAQ on how long it takes to kill a tree stump.)

“The tarp keeps rain out and helps retain heat and moisture, speeding up decomposition,” says Gabe Boersma of Top It or Drop It Tree Service. He advises checking on the treated stump every few weeks, and “within a few months, the stump should be soft enough to break apart with an axe or shovel.”

For smaller tree stumps, you may be able to simply rip the stump and its root system out of the ground using a shovel, a pick, and some elbow grease. (Ask me about the seven or eight hours I spent battling the root structure of a rather small Japanese maple tree in the summer of 2017.) For larger stumps, “our preferred method of removal is to grind the stump,” says Hanley. “A grinder can be used to remove it below grade with minimal site disturbance. We often grind the root system as well. Homeowners can rent a compact stump grinder from a store like Home Depot or Lowe’s, or call your local tree company to grind it.”

Be warned that a stump grinder can be a serious expense. “Renting [a stump grinder] typically costs between $300 and $500, while purchasing one outright can cost approximately $1,500 to $2,000,” says Gottlieb. Before choosing the stump-grinding approach, she advises you to make sure you have adequate access to and space around the stump for the grinding equipment. “Different types of grinders are suited to different stump sizes, and you’ll need enough room to maneuver the machine, along with an electrical hookup to power it.”

Whether you rent or buy a stump grinder or hire professionals to come in and use one for you, this is the most expensive way to remove a tree stump. But it might be worth the money: Stump grinding is by far and away the fastest approach; it can be the most effective, as well.

If you have a chainsaw and feel safe using it, cut the stump as low to the ground as you can. Then clear away as much debris as possible, as the chainsawing process will almost certainly kick up sawdust, fallen twigs, leaves, and loose rocks. Do not let your chainsaw hit the ground! Dirt not only dulls the chain instantly, but it can also cause dangerous kickback, send rocks and other debris flying, and generally be very, very bad. In fact, if there is visible dirt on any part of the stump you’ll be cutting, including roots, rinse off thoroughly with water, then let the stump dry fully before any sawing.

Set the stump grinder in place with its cutting wheel over the stump, then fire it up and slowly lower it down. Once the cutting wheel begins to make contact with the stump, move it from side to side and forward and back as needed to slowly grind away the tree stump.

Use the stump grinder to sever and grind large tree roots you see exposed in the grind-down process; when you’re done grinding both the stump and the root system, be sure to fill the hole you have created with soil, packing it until it’s leveled off. Consider using the ground stump bits as mulch.

It’s important to address the whole root system when killing a tree stump.

If you plan to use fire to kill a tree stump, please, put safety first, second, and third! Make sure there are no overhanging limbs that could combust; cut back and remove nearby grasses, brambles, and underbrush; and have a fire extinguisher ready to deal with any embers that float away.

Despite the many hazards one must be aware of before choosing this tree-stump removal method, there are good arguments to be made for using fire to eliminate your tree stump. “The burning method deserves special attention because it does not require uprooting the stump by hand or using heavy equipment, which can be a challenge if the stump is in a hard-to-reach area,” says Josh Heston, co-owner of ALC Property Maintenance. “The process does not take much time but does require attention to safety.”

Ideally you would do this using a doorknob drill bit or one similar to it, making a large hole in the center of the stump and drilling down as far as you can. Then, with a smaller bit (think a half-inch), drill into the sides of the stump until you reach the main hole. (Take note: This approach will not work with extra-wide stumps, though you may be able to use it by drilling a series of holes down into the top of the stump and connecting to each hole from the side. It’s a process, to be sure, but this approach can be very effective and may be worth trying even for larger tree stumps.)

Taking great care (and wearing gloves), soak the strip or strips of cotton in gas or lighter fluid, then insert all but a few inches of the fabric into the center hole. Switch gloves for safety (be aware of gasoline residue) and then light the fabric. Make sure you are leaning well back from the hole as you apply flame, and remember to wear protective eyewear during this stage; no need for you to end up as a comical internet meme without eyebrows as a result of this process.

“In order for the stump to start burning from the inside, you need to provide an oxygen supply,” says Heston. “For this, periodically blow air through the side holes. Also, from time to time, toss in some dry branches into the central hole to maintain the flame inside.” And do not squirt in gas or lighter fluid; that’s riskier than it’s worth.

It may take several hours for a stump to burn up enough that it can be removed easily with shovels and picks. Even if you don’t plan to fully remove the stump—you just needed to kill it—the burning technique will work and is relatively low-impact.

You don’t need to use fire, harsh chemicals, or heavy machinery to kill a tree stump; you can opt for a much more “natural” approach—but it will take time. Essentially, for the “natural method,” per Boersma, you’ll use “the same equipment as with the chemical [approach], but swap out chemicals for epsom salt or rock salt.”

Epsom salt or rock salt can be used to kill a tree stump.

If you have one, use a chainsaw and cut the stump as low as you safely can. (Again take heed of the notes above on chainsaw stump-removal safety, avoiding the blade from touching dirt or rocks and so on.) Then, drill several wide holes into the stump.

Pour in enough rock or epsom salt to fill each hole you drilled, then pour over enough water to fully saturate the salt. If the salt seems to all wash down into the hole, repeat the process one or two more times, adding more salt, then more water. If a stump is particularly porous (rotting, for example) then you may need to add salt and water two or more times.

Wrap the stump in a durable tarp and secure it in place. Then it’s time to play the waiting game. “This process is slow, taking 6 to 12 months, but is nontoxic and requires very little labor,” says Michael Clarke, the founder of Yardwork. Eventually, the stump will be so broken down that the ground will reclaim it or you can remove it with a shovel.

Our experts stressed the need for caution around chemicals, fire, chainsaws, and the heavy machinery (like stump grinders) one might make use of to kill a tree stump, but safe stump removal also involves really knowing what’s going on under the ground, says Sal Bracale, president of Moon Valley Nurseries. “Always call utility companies before you dig; this is the best advice I would give. Safety is always a concern, so wear proper PPE [personal protective equipment].”

And don’t ever use a brute-force approach to tree-stump removal, Bracale says. “[Killing a tree stump] is a lot of work and effort, but using unsafe techniques like a truck and a chain can cause serious injury if the chain comes off and backlashes toward a home or the truck.” You may well damage or ruin your vehicle. When in doubt, Bracale adds, “Hire a professional who is educated, experienced, licensed, and insured.”

The most common household products you can use to kill tree stumps include epsom salts and rock salts, as noted above, but there are other things you likely have around the house that can be used as well. Like the salts (and stronger herbicidal chemicals), both of the following products should be applied by pouring them down holes you drill into the stump.

You can use undiluted bleach (be careful not to breathe in the fumes) or white vinegar, and make sure to wrap the stump tightly in an airtight tarp after pouring in either of the DIY stump killer fluid. Do not—repeat—do not ever use bleach and vinegar at the same time for anything. Ever. Mixing these two substances can create a dangerous and even deadly gas.

While it could be considered the most labor-intensive and costliest way to fully destroy and remove a tree stump, grinding the stump is the fastest method of removal by far. You can remove a tree stump via grinding in an hour or two, as opposed to the weeks or months it can take to kill off then dig up a stump with chemicals or other products. “Especially for large stumps that need to be removed quickly, it’s best to use a stump grinder,” says Clarke. Larger stumps may resist salts or chemicals, and may continue to resprout new shoots or harbor pests and fungi for a long while.

With a stump grinder, you can kill a tree stump in an hour or two, give or take the preparation and cleanup time. On the far extreme, David Brown, owner of Reliable Tree Service, notes that it can take “six months to two years” to kill off a tree stump via “natural decomposition, [which is] the best approach for eco-conscious or low-budget approaches if you have the time to wait.”

The stump itself won’t damage buried water lines, per se, but the roots can be ruthless with pipes. Emily Demirdonder, cofounder and director of operations and marketing at Proximity Plumbing, says, “Homeowners usually focus on what’s sitting aboveground, but the real risk is what the roots are doing underneath.”

Tree (or stump) roots can spread two to three times the width of the tree’s canopy underground; they grow toward water and nutrients, and clay or PVC pipes are a direct source of both. “Drainage lines are essentially a target,” Demirdonder says. “The stump isn’t safe just because the tree is gone; live roots keep growing for months after removal, and in my work, I’ve tracked root systems traveling over four meters from the original stump during drain repairs. The tree above is dead, but the root network below is still very much active.... I believe if the stump sits within three meters of a drain line, full removal is the right call. Leaving it creates a slow root-intrusion problem that rarely shows surface signs until it’s already serious. The roots infiltrate through tiny joints or cracks, expand from inside, and the pipe collapses gradually. You won’t see it coming until there’s a slow drain or a wet patch in the yard, and by then the damage is usually well underway.”

The most basic reason to kill off a tree stump is to reclaim the space; maybe it’s in the middle of your backyard and the kids want to play there, or it would make for less hassle for you and your lawn mower. Maybe it’s in the middle of your garden and you want more planting space. The list could go on.

But there are other valid reasons that you may want to kill a tree stump—reasons that can protect your home and can protect other trees and plants nearby. “Fungal decay [from a stump] can spread to nearby plants, and the rotting wood often attracts pests,” says Hanley. Adding to that, Boersma explains that “dead stumps can attract pests like termites, carpenter ants, or fungi that can spread to nearby healthy trees or even your home’s structure.”

Yes, a tree can sometimes grow back from a stump, but it depends mainly on the species of the tree and the condition of the roots left behind. When a tree is cut down and its root system remains alive, some species are able to send up new shoots from the stump itself or from the roots, in a natural process called coppicing. Tree species such as oak, willow, maple, poplar, birch, and eucalyptus are especially good at this and can regrow surprisingly quickly, particularly if the tree was relatively young and healthy when it was cut down. On the other hand, most conifers (evergreens like pine, spruce, fir, and cedar) generally do not regrow from stumps because they lack the ability to produce new shoots from old wood, so once they are cut down, the stumps typically die. Other factors also influence whether regrowth happens, including the age of the tree (again, younger trees respond better and tend to grow back), the overall health of the root system, how high the stump was cut, and even the season in which the tree was removed.

As long as that tree stump isn’t putting buried pipes at risk, or acting as a hotbed for pests (and isn’t going to be a pain to mow around), it might be best left right where it is—and that’s true even if it’s rather prominent on your property.

“Removal isn’t the only option; a tree stump can also become part of your landscape design,” says Gottlieb. “Building up a mound of plantings around it offers a way to incorporate the stump into the garden and build on previous iterations of the landscape. The stump itself can serve as a bench, or it can be carved to create an artistic focal point. Leaving it in place can also reduce the disruption to the property that full root removal would cause. Planting around and on the stump, working into the crevices between surface roots, and layering moss and flowering perennials can add depth and visual interest to the garden.”

#5122. What’s at stake for crypto as the US votes in the 2026 midterms?

Crypto groups spent about $176 million ahead of the US midterms, with control of Congress and the future of crypto legislation at stake.

US voters head to the polls in midterm elections on Tuesday that could shift control of Congress and shape the future of cryptocurrency regulation.

Crypto industry groups have spent roughly $176 million across more than 100 races this election cycle, according to an Oct. 27 Reuters report.

Coinbase, Ripple and Andreessen Horowitz are among the main financiers of the Fairshake political network.

The results could reshape the prospects for the stalled CLARITY Act and determine which lawmakers lead committees overseeing the industry.

Republicans enter Tuesday’s midterms controlling both chambers of Congress, with 219 seats in the House of Representatives and 53 in the Senate, according to the Blockchain Association’s election dashboard.

Democrats are seeking to overturn those majorities, with prediction markets favoring the party to win the House and Senate, according to Kalshi data at the time of publishing.

Voters will decide all 435 House seats and 35 Senate races, determining which party controls Congress for the final two years of President Donald Trump’s second term.

Democratic control could bring more congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked crypto businesses and regulators’ decisions. Control of the Senate would also affect confirmation of future presidential nominees to regulatory posts.

Ohio’s Senate race between Republican Jon Husted and Democrat Sherrod Brown is a major test of crypto’s political influence. Industry groups have reportedly spent more than $50 million opposing Brown, a longtime crypto critic who could return to a senior Senate committee role.

In Nebraska, Republican Sen. Pete Ricketts faces independent Dan Osborn. Ricketts, a Senate Banking Committee member who has supported crypto-friendly legislation, has benefited from nearly $11 million in spending by Fairshake, according to the Washington Examiner.

Texas is another race to watch, with Republican Ken Paxton facing Democrat James Talarico in a closely contested Senate election. As Cointelegraph previously reported, the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund has spent money supporting Paxton.

A shift in congressional control could delay the CLARITY Act, although the crypto market structure bill has already struggled under Republican leadership.

The legislation would divide oversight of crypto markets between the Securities and Exchange Commission and the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, as well as establish rules for intermediaries. The Senate failed to advance it in a 49–50 procedural vote on Sept. 15, short of the 60 votes required.

Democratic Sen. Mark Warner cited insufficient safeguards against Trump profiting from his administration’s crypto policies. Republican sponsors said their final draft incorporated substantial ethics concessions sought by Democrats.

“A change in control of Congress would most likely reset the timetable for the CLARITY Act,” Christian Davies, group head of innovation and digital capital at Relm Insurance, told Cointelegraph.

Related: Crypto PAC spending before midterms possibly obscured after reported FEC hack

Ryan Kirkley, CEO of Global Settlement Network, agreed that the challenges extend beyond party control.

“The CLARITY Act was already struggling to get through the Senate with Republicans in control, so clearly the problem goes beyond which party holds the majority,” Kirkley said.

The industry has already secured a federal stablecoin framework through the GENIUS Act, which became law in July 2025.

Bitcoin’s (BTC) price history around US midterms has attracted attention on social media, but analysts caution that past declines offer little guidance on what could happen after Tuesday’s vote.

Bitcoin fell below earlier yearly lows after the 2014, 2018 and 2022 midterms. Source: ManGana

“I’d be careful reading much into Bitcoin’s record after past midterms. There haven’t been enough cycles to call it a pattern, and the market is built differently now: ETF flows, institutional portfolios and global liquidity move the price far more than they used to,” Michael Heinrich, CEO and co-founder of 0G Labs, told Cointelegraph.

Related: From Uptober to Hacktober: Are Bitcoin’s seasonal patterns becoming obsolete?

GSN’s Kirkley said institutional flows, interest rate expectations and market liquidity were more immediate factors affecting Bitcoin.

“Bitcoin doesn’t need a particular election result to succeed. It needs capital, confidence and a macro environment that gives investors a reason to take risk again,” Kirkley said.

#5121. BitGo launches institutional trading and financial solutions business led by former NYDIG executive

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Pete Janney will lead the new BitGo Global Markets division, following the firm's acquisition of NYDIG’s institutional and trading business in August.

Digital asset infrastructure firm BitGo Holdings BitGo Holdings, Inc. (BTGO) announced the launch of BitGo Global Markets on Wednesday, its new institutional trading and financial solutions business bringing together global liquidity, execution, financing, derivatives, and settlement services.

The division will be headed up by former NYDIG executive Pete Janney following BitGo's acquisition of NYDIG's institutional and trading business on Aug. 27, according to a statement shared exclusively with The Block.

About 30 employees joined BitGo following the NYDIG acquisition, bringing experience in derivatives, financing, collateral servicing, and structured products. Janney initially came aboard as head of financial infrastructure and brings extensive experience in trading and capital markets.

BitGo has been offering institutional trading through BitGo Prime since 2020, with that business becoming part of Global Markets alongside the expanded derivatives, collateral management, and structured finance solutions from NYDIG.

"BitGo has built a strong foundation across custody, liquidity, trading, financing, and settlement," BitGo CEO Mike Belshe said in the statement. "Global Markets brings these capabilities together with deeper capital markets expertise, allowing us to support more complex institutional strategies and serve a broader range of our clients' financial needs. Pete understands how institutions trade and manage risk, and I'm excited to have him leading Global Markets.”

Under the new unit, BitGo's Global Liquidity Layer will provide access to spot trading and large over-the-counter transactions, drawing liquidity from exchanges, market makers, and other counterparties, the firm said. Clients can also borrow against their crypto holdings to fund trading positions, with BitGo keeping the collateral in qualified custody, monitoring margins, and handling collateral calls or liquidations when needed.

Additionally, BitGo Global Markets will offer options, swaps, and custom structured products for eligible counterparties to hedge exposure, express market views, and manage portfolio risk, the firm said.

Global Markets will also provide managed investment strategies, private funds, and research, while BitGo's Go Network will handle clearing and settlement.

"Clients who own digital assets need sophisticated solutions that go beyond buy and hold," Janney said. "I look forward to accelerating that work and growing the Global Markets franchise at BitGo."

BTGO was trading up 3.4% in pre-market trading on Wednesday, according to TradingView, following a 3.7% rise on Tuesday.

#5118. Circle reports $865 million in Q3 revenue and reserve income, discloses 5% workforce reduction

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Circle booked $182 million in ARC token presale revenue during the quarter, while income from its USDC reserves fell.

Stablecoin issuer Circle Internet Group Circle Internet Group, Inc. (CRCL) reported $865 million in total revenue and reserve income for the third quarter, up 17% from a year earlier, according to its earnings release on Wednesday.

Net income from continuing operations came in at $226 million, compared with $214.4 million in the same quarter last year. Adjusted EBITDA was $317 million, up from $171.5 million in Q3 2025. Circle previously reported $701 million in revenue and reserve income for the second quarter of this year, naming BlackRock, DTCC, and Visa among founding validators for its Arc blockchain.

Operating expenses rose 16% year over year to $245 million in the third quarter, the firm said, citing higher administrative, IT infrastructure, and marketing costs. Distribution, transaction, and other costs fell 8% to $412 million, mostly due to lower distribution payments, it added.

A footnote to the earnings tables also disclosed that Circle had reduced its workforce by approximately 5%, with the company booking $9.1 million in associated restructuring expenses, mainly for notice period and severance payments, employee benefits, and related costs. Circle said the reduction was designed to "better align our organizational structure with our operating model and strategic priorities," but did not give a headcount for the cuts. Circle had approximately 1,100 employees worldwide at the end of 2025, according to its annual report.

Of the $207 million Circle reported in other revenue, $182 million was recognized in Q3 from its ARC token presale. Other revenue had totaled $29 million in the third quarter of 2025. Circle agreed to sell a total of $242.2 million worth of ARC tokens in the presale at a purchase price of $0.30 per token and a $3 billion fully diluted valuation. Circle attributed the 85% increase in adjusted EBITDA to the presale revenue and lower distribution costs, partly offset by spending on new products.

Circle also maintained its full-year 2026 guidance, including $310 million to $330 million in other revenue and $570 million to $585 million in adjusted operating expenses.

Reserve income fell 8% year over year to $658 million as its reserve return rate declined by 61 basis points, the firm said. Circle's USDC stablecoin averaged $73.3 billion in circulation during Q3, an 8% increase from a year earlier. Onchain USDC transaction volume reached $13.9 trillion for the quarter, up 49% year over year. USDC circulation stood at $73.9 billion at quarter-end, unchanged from a year earlier, while Circle's stablecoin market share fell to 27%.

Circle opened Arc's public mainnet on Sept. 16, with more than 100 institutional and ecosystem builders on the network at launch. It also minted the initial supply of 10 billion ARC tokens, which the firm said was a technical milestone, not a commitment to launch them publicly. It expects Arc to move from proof of authority to proof of stake in 2027.

In September, Circle also agreed to acquire Tazapay, a Singapore-based payments firm with more than $25 billion in annualized payment volume. The firm expects the Tazapay acquisition to close in 2027, subject to regulatory approvals. Circle Ventures had led Tazapay's $36 million Series B extension in March. Binance also bought $100 million worth of Circle shares in September and signed a new five-year USDC agreement.

#5117. Where Does Christine Baranski Live? Exploring the Gilded Age Star’s Real Estate Portfolio

Her Gilded Age character lives in a grand Upper East Side brownstone, but where does Christine Baranski live? The Emmy- and Tony–winning actor was born in Buffalo, New York, and still owns property in her home state in the form of a Manhattan pied-à-terre—and yes, like Agnes van Rhijn’s onscreen dwelling, it is on the Upper East Side. However, the Good Wife star primarily lives in Connecticut, where her late husband grew up and where the couple raised their two daughters, Isabel and Lily.

Read on to learn more about Baranski’s property portfolio.

For decades, Baranski has lived in Connecticut’s Litchfield County. Her first visit to the region was on a 1982 motorcycle ride; her future husband, fellow actor Matthew Cowles, was the driver. Cowles grew up in an 18th-century farmhouse in the small town of Bethlehem. “I had such a feeling of destiny about it,” Baranski recalled in a 2008 interview, “I loved it. I loved the smells in the air, the farms, the field. It was a dream. When Matthew took me into his home, I thought, ‘We’re gonna raise children here.’” The couple married the next year, briefly residing in an NYC apartment before returning to raise their daughters, born in 1984 and in 1987, in Cowles’s childhood home. “I had such a sense of eternity, like I was meant to be there,” she added.

A 2003 interview reported that Baranski primarily lived in Bethlehem and maintained a lake house about half an hour away, and it seems that this is still the case. “I have a marvelous house on a lake in Connecticut and I’ve hosted some very famous actors there,” the actor said in a recent interview with The Guardian, adding that those famed visitors have, on occasion, stripped naked for a swim. “It has a very long dock that has been ‘grandfathered’ in, as in they don’t allow a dock that long to be built anymore, but this is about 100 years old. You sit in the living room in summer and watch the sun set, and then go out into the evening and skinny dip in the lake.”

Cowles died in 2014, but Baranski remains deeply rooted in his hometown. The Mamma Mia! star is so entrenched in the community that she became active in the Housatonic Valley Association, a local environmental nonprofit, and currently serves as one of its directors. “I enjoy so much the beauty of Litchfield County,” Baranski said in 2009. “I couldn't imagine living in a more beautiful place.”

In 2004, Baranski bought a pied-à-terre on Manhattan’s Upper East Side for $1.27 million. Located in a 1920s structure where Jackie Kennedy lived as a child, the two-bedroom, two-bathroom apartment featured a 21-foot-long living room with hardwood floors, a wood-burning fireplace flanked by built-in bookshelves, and yellow-striped wallpaper. A galley kitchen opened to a small dining room painted a soft sage green and decorated with elegant paintings in ornate gold frames, while the bedrooms were done up in distinct palettes: one was blue and white, while the other was tan with pops of red and orange. Both had their own en suite bath.

“Happily, now after years of commuting to LA and trying to do the whole bicoastal thing, The Good Wife shoots in Greenpoint, Brooklyn,” Baranski said in a 2011 New York Times article. “Our kids are grown up and in graduate school, so I have this life where I can enjoy the city and not worry about racing home to Connecticut.” The actor shared her typical Sunday routine in the city, which included reading in a café, visiting museums, and seeing friends. “I tend to call Sundays a girlfriend day because my husband is in Connecticut, so more often than not I’m alone here, which is a chance to catch up with girlfriends,” she said. “I might do a lunch at one of the hole-in-the-wall French bistros in the neighborhood, but sometimes I skip lunch in favor of an early dinner.” Then, the actor turns in early. “If I’m in town on a Sunday, it usually means I’m working Monday, and often I’m the first shot of the day, which means you’re up at 4:30. That means going to bed early. So I’ll go to my early dinner and then try to get myself to sleep by 9 or 9:30.”

Inhabitants of the co-op building, designed by architect Lafayette A. Goldstone, have access to a backyard garden, a gym, and a laundry room. Baranski listed her unit in the nine-story building in February 2021 for $1.39 million, and it sold that June.

Baranski didn’t move too far, however. In December 2020, the Good Fight star upgraded to a larger $2.2 million duplex unit in the same building. Listing photos showed that the home was sold with a few spaces under construction, so she likely added some custom touches before moving in. The apartment has four bedrooms, three bathrooms, and a much larger eat-in kitchen than the one found in Baranski’s previous unit. It still boasts a wood-burning fireplace in the living room, accessed from the foyer through an arched doorway. On its other end, the living room opens to a spacious formal dining room. A guest bedroom rounds out the lower level. The remaining bedrooms are at the top of a winding staircase. Another fireplace, tucked between built-ins, graces one bedroom, which shares a Jack-and-Jill bathroom with another bedroom—which could be used as a dressing room—that boasts six closets. The final bedroom has a full en suite bathroom.

#5115. Brazil's Biggest Bitcoin Exchange Says It Will Refund Users After a $40 Million Hack

Brazil's Biggest Bitcoin Exchange Says It Will Refund Users After a $40 Million Hack

Brazil's largest crypto exchange says its users won't pay for its hack. Mercado Bitcoin, which lost tens of millions of dollars on Monday, says the losses are its problem, not its customers'.

An exchange holds your coins the way a bank holds your cash, and when one gets robbed, the customers are often the ones left waiting to see what they get back.

The exchange spotted a breach early on November 2 and froze deposits and withdrawals. “We identified and quickly contained a security incident in one of our systems early this morning,” Mercado Bitcoin said in a Portuguese-language statement on X. “As a preventive protection measure, deposits and withdrawals are temporarily suspended.”

It said no customer lost money and no data leaked. It has not said how the attackers got in.

How much was taken depends on who is counting. Security firm Hypernative flagged more than $40 million in suspicious transfers across at least 10 blockchains, including Bitcoin, Ethereum, Solana, and Tron.

Primeiro, o mais importante: os ativos dos clientes estão garantidos . Os saldos continuam registrados nas respectivas contas.

Ontem (2/11), identificamos um incidente de segurança. Acionamos na hora nossos protocolos de segurança e governança e informamos as autoridades.…

— Mercado Bitcoin (@MercadoBitcoin) November 3, 2026

The exchange's own number is lower. “Right now the value of the assets involved is about $38 million,” Mercado Bitcoin said in a Portuguese-language update on X. “After a quick action with the crypto community and strategic partners, including Tether, Circle, Paxos, BRL1 and Chainalysis, we have already blocked $11 million.”

Others put it higher. Blockchain analysts at Arkham traced at least $46 million, while Chainalysis says about $49 million, with nearly $10 million frozen. The exchange has not said whether its $38 million includes the frozen amount.

Tether, which issues the USDT stablecoin (a token pegged to the dollar), froze more than $4.5 million in USDT tied to the incident, CEO Paolo Ardoino said on X.

A freeze only stops the tokens from moving. It doesn't return them to their owner, which is why the exchange's promise to cover losses carries the weight here.

On October 29, hackers drained more than $6 billion from Binance, the world's biggest exchange, and the FBI blamed North Korea's TraderTraitor group. Mercado Bitcoin has not linked its incident to that attack.

Binance made the same pledge as Mercado Bitcoin. “Binance will absorb the financial impact of the stolen funds, not our users,” CEO Richard Teng wrote on X, adding that about 95% of the affected assets had been replenished.

Neither promise can be checked from the outside. A guarantee to cover losses is only as good as the money behind it, and exchanges don't have to show customers their books.

For now, Mercado Bitcoin deposits and withdrawals remain suspended with no restart date. The exchange says it will post updates on X and resume service once full security is restored.

#5111. 10 Best Bedside Lamps & Table Lamps (2026)

Before you spin into a redecorating frenzy (step away from the chandeliers…), consider the transformative power of the table lamp. The best bedside lamps are a necessary lighting addition to any bedroom—nighttime reader or not. They also have staying power away from the bedside, should they find their home in another part of the house as your taste shifts: add them to a shelf on a bookcase, show them off in an entryway, let one adorn your kitchen countertop.

Accent lighting has always been a thing, but lately, with the rise of anti–Big Light content on TikTok and Reels, the demand for cute, trendy ambient lighting options is going way up. And we get it—relying on built-in overhead light fixtures feels cold and corporate, while smaller lamps create a warmer ambience. That’s why we had the bright idea to test a curated selection of top picks from some of our favorite brands (in addition to other great options that score high design marks in our books). In each of our reviews you’ll find an AD editor’s testimony as to what makes each light stand out, how they use it in their space, and what’s something buyers should keep in mind. The hardest part now will be deciding on just one (or two).

(Lamps pictured with a 2.4-ounce candle for size comparison)

The head is magnetized, so you can direct light in pretty much any direction you choose.

Flos’s Bilboquet Table Lamp, designed by Philippe Malouin, is another seen-before decor moment, as we tapped it for our 2025 gifting shoot. It’s been a mainstay at Fletcher’s work station ever since. “This sexy little lamp has a great glow and looks just as handsome when it isn't turned on,” she told us. “I keep it at my desk and it's a lovely distraction from the standard issue fluorescents here at 1WTC. I love that the bulbous chrome joint is a functional swivel point, which means it can spotlight almost 360 degrees.” An eco-friendly design as well, Flos crafted the lamp’s body from a byproduct of paper production and refrained from using any glue, so the components will be recyclable after a long life spent spotlighting your home.

A built-in dimmer switch allows you to customize the glow.

The switch requires a substantial turn to activate and may not be the best choice for illuminating a full workspace. If the Raffia Wave shade isn’t your style, there’s three other shade options available, along with a range of colored bases.

Looking for some living room table lighting with a touch of coastal grandma style? Look no further than this rattan lamp from Serena and Lily. With its petite size, the light can fill up empty nooks and crannies while perfectly illuminating them. “What immediately caught my eye was how the unique, trendy raffia shade complements the sleek ceramic base beautifully,” associate visuals editor Shani Kravetz told us. “The lamp is the perfect size for a desk or kitchen counter, adding a warm, ambient glow and delightful pop of style.”

A button controls this lamp's four-step dimmer.

The adjustable top on this geometric lamp allows you to direct the light wherever you need it.

Sheds more of an ambient glow rather than the direct light a task lamp might provide.

The Lagos sheds a warm white light perfect for winding down and doing a bit of reading (ie: scrolling) before lights out. The red base and white shade would look particularly cute bedside, especially next to a striped bedding setup.

While you can use a higher watt light bulb than your light fixture suggests, we wouldn’t recommend it. Wattage relates to how much power a particular device uses, and if more than the design allows is being used, you face the risk of ‘overlamping,’ meaning your fixture will start to run particularly hot. This is bad for a few reasons: Overheating can melt bulb sockets and wiring and, in the worst cases, lead to an electrical fire. It may also cost you more in the long run and be less energy-efficient—if your bulbs are drawing too much energy, it’ll likely mean having to replace them more frequently. If you’re still confused on the correct bulb to look for, we hope this handy visual guide will help.

There are many factors we considered for each of our reviews to give you the insight you need to select the best table lamps for your home, namely the following:

Brand snobs: While thousands of companies produce lighting, it’s hard to know who won’t try to sell you dollhouse-sized decor or a fixture with faulty wiring. That’s why we’ve used our brand expertise and design discernment to bring you options from labels you can trust (because we trust them enough to buy from them ourselves!). From brands like West Elm and Wayfair who also produce a sensational sectional or bookshelf to more indie names like Gantri, who specialize in the all things lighting.

Delivery and assembly: Did our package arrive within the guaranteed 10-day delivery window? Was the delicate porcelain base carefully packed with the necessary cushioning? Did the instruction manual for assembly look like it could have been ripped out of an astrophysics textbook? We wanted to let you know exactly what to expect from your purchases and called out anything surprising. We also double-checked if a brand provided you with a complimentary bulb or if it’s something you should add to your next Amazon order.

Sizing: While we provide specs on exact measurements in all these reviews, sizing can still be a difficult factor to gauge. To help you determine whether or not that CB2 lamp will totally overwhelm your nightstand as well as help you compare the lamps of this list, we shot each close-up with a 2.4-ounce candle as a base point for size comparison.

What makes this lamp unique? Does the lamp’s design fit a clear aesthetic? Does it function particularly well in a specific area of the house? Incredibly easy to use? Our reviews were designed to give insight on why each lamp could be the right lamp for your Scandi oasis or midcentury abode. We also wrote about any ingenious details that show how thoughtful the designer was as well as why the piece was just what our tester was looking for.

What to keep in mind: Nobody’s perfect, and beauty can be in the eye of the beholder—which is why each lamp also has a note detailing what a potential buyer should keep in mind. For example, if assembly was particularly difficult, the lamp is extremely delicate or hard to clean, and who the design might not be suited for.

#5110. Bitcoin price defends $76K lows as analysts see stocks pricing in ‘political gridlock’

Bitcoin made modest gains at the start of Wall Street trading on Tuesday as markets awaited cues from the US midterm elections.

Bitcoin (BTC) headed higher at Tuesday’s Wall Street open as a tech-stock rebound under the shadow of uncertainty surrounding the US midterm elections.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD circling $77,000, continuing to retain local lows near $76,300 seen on Friday.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Stocks faced conflicting forces at the start of the US trading session. Apple shares sought to pare losses after falling 4% in premarket trading as the company forecast gross margins of 45% to 46% for the December quarter, down from 47.9% in the previous quarter, citing rising memory-chip costs.

Markets also weighed the potential impact of the US midterm elections on the balance of power in Congress. Although polls suggested significant Democratic gains, some analysts cautioned that the results could be less decisive than expected.

Apple stock one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

“Investors seem to have a significant dose of skepticism about whether the final outcome is going to be quite as favorable to Democrats as the polling suggests,” Tobin Marcus, head of US policy and politics at equity research company Wolfe Research, told CNBC. Wolfe added that polls were “likely overstating Democratic strength.”

The S&P 500 and tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite Index finished Monday down 0.5% and 1.1%, respectively. Analysts at trading resource The Kobeissi Letter argued that US political uncertainty, rather than concerns over AI spending alone, was driving market weakness.

“The reality is that the market is pricing-in a major shift in the political landscape of the US,” it wrote on X.

The analysis suggested that a divided Congress could lead to frequent legislative impasses, creating conditions in which policy decisions would become less predictable.

“A change in the political landscape always comes with uncertainty; markets hate uncertainty,” it continued.

Nasdaq Composite Index one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Related: Bitcoin price threatens $77.7K support loss with recent buyers underwater

Oil prices meanwhile brushed off a US attack on Iran’s Qeshm Island, a key military control outpost for the Strait of Hormuz oil route.

The move came just days after US Secretary of State Marco Rubio said that President Donald Trump was open to resolving the US-Iran war “diplomatically,” comments that triggered a 7% drop in WTI crude oil on Monday.

WTI initially jumped to $98.80 per barrel before dipping below $94 on Tuesday, hitting its lowest level since Oct. 15.

CFDs on WTI crude oil one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

#5109. CFTC Lets Traders Bet on Stocks With Crypto-Style Perpetual Futures

CFTC Lets Traders Bet on Stocks With Crypto-Style Perpetual Futures

The U.S. derivatives regulator has opened the door to perpetual futures on individual stocks, importing one of crypto’s most popular trading instruments into traditional equity markets for the first time.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission said Tuesday that “equity perpetual futures,” or perpetual contracts tied to individual stocks, can now begin trading on CFTC-registered exchanges.

Perpetual futures, long a staple of crypto markets, are derivatives that track an asset’s price but carry no expiration date, letting traders hold leveraged positions indefinitely as long as they meet margin and funding requirements. Until now, they’ve been largely off-limits to U.S. traders.

Chairman Michael Selig framed the move as the latest step in a campaign to bring perpetual products onshore.

“In my inaugural remarks, I directed the CFTC to use the tools at its disposal to onshore perpetual derivative products so that they can flourish in the U.S., subject to appropriate safeguards,” he said, calling the stock contracts an extension of the agency’s earlier approval of the first true Bitcoin perpetual futures on a regulated U.S. exchange.

That milestone, as Decrypt reported, saw the CFTC clear Bitcoin perpetual futures for Kalshi earlier this year.

Selig returned to a familiar argument: that regulated U.S. venues are safer than the offshore platforms traders have otherwise had to use.

Bringing these products into regulated markets “ensures that American investors and traders can access these products within the protections of the CFTC’s proven regulatory framework, rather than being forced to turn to offshore, and often unregulated, venues,” he said.

It’s a theme he pressed just last week when he used the record Binance hack to argue offshore exchanges need U.S. oversight.

The equity perps were deemed approved as security futures products under the Commodity Futures Modernization Act of 2000, placing them under the joint oversight of the CFTC and the Securities and Exchange Commission.

Selig credited a historic memorandum of understanding the two agencies signed in March for ending the jurisdictional limbo that had, in SEC Chairman Paul Atkins’s words, stranded such products in a regulatory “no man’s land.”

The approval arrives amid a rush to bring perps onshore. The CFTC has floated plans to bring crypto exchanges under federal oversight, dismissed a CME lawsuit challenging crypto perpetual futures, and cleared the way for Coinbase, which recently filed to list single-stock perps on names like Apple, Tesla and Nvidia.

For Selig, the goal is explicit: ensuring “the new frontier of finance is built in the United States of America.”

#5102. Quince’s $190 Quilt Set Is Worth Every Dollar

Since its 2018 founding, Quince’s enormous catalog and affordable prices have captured national attention, leading many a shopper to wonder whether the brand was too good to be true. The shopping team at AD set out for an answer by putting Quince’s full lineup to the test. Ultimately, we found 17 favorite items.

The direct-to-consumer retailer’s bamboo sheets are among our favorite sets on the market, and its linen sheets get top marks for that bedding category. Quince’s curtains and towels, too, have made us fans, and its velvet sectional received high praise from our audience development manager.

Given the brand’s well-established pattern of creating buy-worthy textiles and furniture, I was excited to test its newest fall bedding collection. As a quilt connoisseur, I opted to start with the European Linen Cotton Stitch Quilt Set—with shams, obviously. In light of the thermostat-swinging nature of the fall weather, I hoped the quilt set could function as a trans-seasonal solution and give my room a noticeable (and needed) refresh.

Quilts are often heavy, so I was surprised by how lightweight the package felt before I even unboxed it. As it turns out, the quilt is light by design, constructed from a combination of linen and cotton, with cotton fill that weighs just 200 grams per square meter (gsm)—or about the same as a flannel shirt by the square meter. I was relieved by its lack of heft, as my New York apartment’s heat runs on radiators that I can’t control and is, as a result, basically blazing hot at all times. Upon situating the quilt on my bed, I immediately searched the pick-stitching for loose threads. Devastatingly, I found some—a few pulled threads around the border—though not so exaggerated that I’d need to make my bed with caution.

The quilt set is framed by jade lamps and maroon shades to give surrounding color.

My experience with this quilt set was, in a word, peaceful. The best quilts offer a little warmth, a little weight, and you can comfortably pull them up and over you without struggling or yanking. All of that is possible with this Quince style. I like sleeping totally wrapped in this quilt. Because of the aforementioned radiator situation, it would be unbearably hot if I tried pairing this with a comforter, so I had only a flat sheet between myself and the quilt, which I found to be the perfect pairing. Its low-loft, pick-stitch construction and weight made it extremely easy to create a crisp bedscape. Its texture also disguises sheet wrinkles and lies super flat without much effort at all. I especially appreciate this quality in the shams, since my pillows usually look wrinkled and misshapen. And I do recommend opting for the bundle with the shams, which keep things looking put together.

After the quilt had a spin through the laundry, I noticed no differences in its soft feel. It came out of the dryer wrinkle-free, without any hint of being frayed or fried. And that’s saying something, as I battle-tested this quilt in a New York laundromat where it endured high heat and no option for a gentle cycle.

At $190 for a total bed refresh, this high-quality set is a nice buy. I love how it lies, and I believe it’ll be a big win for anyone who hates making their bed. The quilt set makes the center of my room look polished, but not stuffy. It skews a bit casual for my maximal taste, but I love the warm ivory option for a great guest-room spread, where I keep things more uniform and less lavish. Ultimately, the weight, the feel, and the price point make it one of the most solid quilt-set choices I’ve come across in my very diligent searching.

#5100. The 60 Best White Elephant Gift Ideas of 2026 Are Worth the Steal

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

A White Elephant gift exchange can go so many ways. First, you have to find the perfect present to delight your friends, horrify your family, and prank your colleagues. Then comes the gift-stealing chaos and the excitement of what you'll ultimately take home. If that makes you skeptical, trust that it's FUN.

For those who haven't had the pleasure, White Elephant is a holiday game where everyone brings a gift—usually with a set price limit. The gifts are usually a little bit off-color, though not always. But it's not another game of Secret Santa. Instead, White Elephant kicks off with someone picking a present at random, so whatever you gift has to have pretty wide appeal.

From there, everyone can either steal a gift that's already been opened or pick a new one. Whether you can steal gifts back is a key distinction before things get competitive and you end up with a bunch of Grinches on your hands.

#5098. Crypto Docs Host GitBook Hit by AI Agent Security Breach, Urges Users to Rotate Keys

Crypto Docs Host GitBook Hit by AI Agent Security Breach, Urges Users to Rotate Keys

GitBook, a documentation platform widely used by cryptocurrency projects, has disclosed a security breach involving AI agents that allowed an attacker to gain access to some of its infrastructure credentials, forcing the company to shut down services and revoke customer access tokens.

In a security disclosure published today, the company described the incident as “a sophisticated, highly targeted attack using AI agents.” The attacker gained unauthorized access at 7 p.m. Central European Time on November 2, according to GitBook, which said it detected the intrusion immediately and moved to contain it.

The reference to AI agents is significant. Unlike traditional chatbots that simply respond to questions, AI agents can carry out sequences of actions, interact with software, and perform tasks with limited human supervision, making them potentially useful to attackers trying to automate parts of a cyberattack. Some are even capable of acting autonomously and even against their guidelines, as OpenAI’s and Anthropic’s experiments have proven.

GitBook has not explained exactly how the attacker used these tools, which AI systems were involved, or how the credentials were obtained. Its disclosure also does not establish that GitBook's own AI products were responsible for the intrusion.

The company took its application and documentation sites requiring authentication offline while securing its infrastructure. It also kept publicly available documentation accessible through cached copies for as long as possible, allowing visitors to continue reading previously saved versions of pages even while the underlying systems were unavailable.

A separate status notice acknowledged that GitBook was experiencing downtime affecting its published documentation sites and editor. By Tuesday afternoon, GitBook said its application, API, and published documentation were accessible again, although some services could remain degraded during recovery.

GitBook is effectively an online instruction manual for software products. Cryptocurrency projects use platforms like it to publish technical explanations, developer instructions, and guides that help users understand everything from connecting software to interacting with decentralized financial applications.

The documents themselves are often public, but the systems that maintain them can connect to private accounts and external services. Those connections depend on credentials that are supposed to remain secret, and GitBook says some of them may have been exposed.

Its investigation identified several potentially affected categories, including integration tokens, Git Sync secrets used to connect documentation with code repositories, and API tokens that allow software to communicate with GitBook automatically.

The incident has already prompted a response from the cryptocurrency industry, where documentation websites often serve as an important source of official instructions.

Decentralized exchange PancakeSwap temporarily disabled its documentation following news of the incident, although it said its own systems had not been compromised.

In a public statement, PancakeSwap said its “docs are not compromised and [the] PancakeSwap app is not affected.” The exchange nevertheless took its documentation offline as a precaution and redirected visitors to its homepage.

PancakeSwap reminded users that its documentation does not require them to perform wallet transactions, warning that “docs never ask you to connect your wallet or approve tokens.”

PancakeSwap subsequently announced that its documentation was back online after being moved from GitBook to infrastructure under its own control, with existing documentation links continuing to work.

GitBook says it blocked the attacker, addressed the vulnerabilities involved, replaced its own infrastructure credentials, and invalidated customer API tokens. But customers using GitBook alongside other services still have work to do.

The company's instructions begin with “rotate any existing GitBook API tokens,” meaning customers should replace their old digital access keys with newly generated ones.

GitBook also instructed customers to replace third-party API tokens and secrets used to connect external tools to its platform, including credentials associated with published integrations. Users of its Model Context Protocol, or MCP, server must authenticate again; MCP is a system that allows AI assistants and other software tools to connect to services such as GitBook.

#5097. Binance traces $6.3B hack to fake job interviews, compromised AI tool

Hackers exploited compromised employee computers and stolen credentials to breach Binance’s wallet infrastructure, according to the exchange’s investigation.

A coding assignment from a prospective employer seemed like an ordinary part of a job interview. Instead, it was one step in a months-long cyberattack that ended with $6.3 billion stolen from Binance, raising questions about how exchanges protect themselves against threats that target their employees.

The attack was part of a coordinated campaign that exploited both human vulnerabilities and internal security systems, according to a postmortem released by Binance on Tuesday. The exchange’s findings detailed how hackers gradually gained access to its wallet infrastructure before the Oct. 29 theft.

According to Binance’s security report, the attack campaign began in June, when an engineer downloaded a tampered software package while setting up an AI coding tool. Although Binance detected the malware and replaced the affected device, the attackers had already gathered information about the exchange’s wallet team.

In September, attackers posing as recruiters targeted another software engineer and tricked the employee into downloading a malicious file disguised as a coding assignment. The malware compromised the engineer’s work computer and evaded the exchange’s security protections.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used the compromised computer and an internal AI tool to gain access to two additional engineers’s systems, obtaining deployment credentials that allowed them to install malicious software within Binance’s wallet infrastructure on Oct. 29.

The attackers then gained access to the exchange’s internal wallet-signing service, which authorizes cryptocurrency transactions. This allowed them to move funds outside the normal withdrawal process.

“This wasn’t one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign,” Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said in a Tuesday post on X.

Binance said the attackers also exploited Ethereum’s EIP-7702 mechanism, which allows wallets to delegate transaction permissions to smart contracts, enabling unauthorized transfers to continue even after the exchange suspended withdrawals. Its multiparty computation (MPC) wallet system, which distributes control over transaction signing, complicated efforts to revoke those permissions.

The breach affected approximately $6.3 billion in digital assets, with Binance reporting that $2.85 billion had been frozen or intercepted, according to its latest accounting. The exchange said it would absorb the losses rather than pass them on to customers and had replenished 98% of the affected token assets as of Oct. 31.

The attack, the largest cryptocurrency hack in history, has been attributed to North Korean hackers by the Federal Bureau of Investigation (FBI). On Tuesday, Kim Yo Jong, the sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong Un, denied the country’s involvement, saying the FBI’s attribution was politically motivated and intended to damage North Korea’s international standing.

Binance co-founder Yi He said Tuesday that the exchange’s security team had detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, including fraudulent job interviews, malicious software packages and social engineering attempts aimed at stealing credentials or installing malware.

“We intercepted many of them, but the cost of failing to stop even one was severe—and this should never have happened,” she said.

Magazine: Binance hackers raced to move billions before it could be frozen

#5095. 7 Best Throw Blanket Picks for Cozy Nights, Tested by AD (2026)

With cool evenings underway, and a list of books to keep me sane throughout winter, I’ve done my due diligence of putting together a comprehensive throw blanket list for a cozy night in. I’ve rounded up my personal favorites and asked other Shop AD writers which ones they love for movie marathons, napping, and working from home.

Our top picks are made from quality materials like Grade-A Mongolian cashmere and merino wool that are smooth and soft without feeling too heavy for lounging. The design factor was just as important in our research. As much as I love a neutral blanket that can blend in any background, these blankets come in a range of colors and patterns to fit different interior styles—some even created by design icons like Alexander Girard. I’ve prioritized machine-washable options, with a couple of worthy exceptions, and included care instructions so you know what to expect on laundry day. Browse all of the best blankets below.

This throw’s “Matisse” name is fitting because it’s a true masterpiece. The wool-blend blanket has made the rounds across the Shop AD team’s desks all year since it’s just as vibrant as it is thoughtfully made. It’s designed by Ezcaray, a Spain-based, family-owned and -operated textile house, known for artisan-made pieces like this one that are hand-washed, -dyed, and -woven. I had it for a while at my desk and can confirm that the “prairie sunrise” blanket’s colors look rich like pictured online. I wouldn’t consider myself a maximalist by any means, but I do appreciate the “more is more” color combinations and how easy it would be to style this in an eclectic bedroom filled with collected decor. In terms of texture, I’d say it almost feels like a goldendoodle’s fur. The fabric is soft like you’d expect, but it almost has a silkiness to it, likely because of the mohair added in the blend. The best part is that it has held up beautifully all year long with no signs of shedding.

If you want a blanket that’s soft without feeling weighty, you can’t go wrong with a good cashmere throw. I always look out for Grade-A Mongolian cashmere in particular since its fine, long fibers are more durable than cheaper cashmeres, like Grade-C, or wool blends. My colleague Lauren Arzbaecher, Shop AD shopping producer, put Quince’s version on my radar last year after talking about it so enthusiastically at the office. She told me it felt “insanely soft” and always kept her comfortably “bundled up while reading a good book.”

My take? Everything Arzbaecher told me was true plus more. It feels breathable enough to keep me warm without sweating on the nights my apartment radiator is cranked up too high. I have the blanket in a deep moss color that fits my moody home design, but there are a dozen other colors, so you can find what works best for your space.

Even though I’ve tried my fair share of soft throw blankets in my life, this one is a favorite since it hits a trifecta of sorts: It’s incredibly fluffy, warm, and easy to care for. It’s made of 100% faux mohair, but Pom Pom at Home has me fooled. The blanket has a brushed finish that gives the blanket an ultrasoft feel, so much so that it’s quickly become my cold-weather companion this year. Ever since the temperature dipped below 50 degrees, I’ve kept it draped on my sofa for countless naps and cozy movie nights.

Fair warning: I’m quite gentle with this blanket, but I could see it easily snagging on sharp furniture corners if you’re not mindful. I haven’t noticed general material shedding in the last couple of months I’ve had the blanket, so that’s a good sign for longevity, especially since it’s a fuzzier-looking fabric.

Of course, I’d prefer it if it were made of pure mohair, but the fact that it resembles the material without looking or feeling cheap is a huge win. I’d recommend this piece to anyone who wants a plushy blanket without paying a steep price.

Architect and interior designer Alexander Girard wore a few hats during his career, one of them being a textile designer for Braniff International Airways. He designed this blanket as part of a 17,543-item brand-identity system for the airline, known for its bold design, in 1965. Today, Pendleton re-creates the iconic multi-stripe design, and we think it’s a great choice for anyone looking for a personality piece. “As a design enthusiast, no surface of mine goes unadorned—even a lowly desk chair deserves a decorative blanket, especially in a chilly office like Condé Nast’s with the AC blasting,” says Arzbaecher. “This striped wool throw rests over the back of my office chair, providing extra cushion and much-needed color.”

The design is understandably a huge draw, but its texture deserves just as much attention. It’s a smooth wool blend (82% wool and 18% cotton), and Arzbaecher does not find it the least bit “scratchy or too thick to the touch.”

Contributor Yelena Alpert considers this her travel blanket—it moves from her bed to the family room couch, and everyone fights over who gets to curl up under it. “The grand waffle texture is certainly noteworthy, but it is the warmth factor that makes this throw a winner,” she says, adding that it feels like her favorite cable-knit sweater. “Since it is made from merino wool, your body is immediately ensconced as if in a hug. It does not feel scratchy or itchy, in case you have a fear of wool.” The knotted fringe at the end of the throw might seem like a minimal detail, but Alpert says it makes her sectional look even cozier to snuggle up on.

“I am also a huge fan of the camel color,” she says. “It pairs so beautifully with my plum matelassé duvet cover, but also enriches the rosewood hue of my velvet quilt.”

My pretty-in-pink throw blanket has never failed to put a smile on my face in the last year. Its salmon color is vibrant and warm without going full Barbiecore. It’s also the most detailed herringbone blanket I’ve ever had—for those who are new to this design too, the pattern has a distinct V shape that looks like the skeleton of a herring fish. Sadly, some of the little loops have snagged on my watch as I’ve unwrapped myself to get up for a lunch break, so it’s something to think about if you’re worried about a pet clawing at your blanket.

The throw is another mainstay at my office desk when I need to thaw out after a cold subway commute, and it hasn’t disappointed. I’ve even lent it to my coworkers who could use an extra layer during the day, and they say it’s warm without feeling stuffy.

This is technically a bed blanket, so the size ranges from a Twin to a California King, but it doesn’t have the typical bulk of a big bedspread. Alpert likes the blanket since it’s lightweight and folds pretty flat when she places it at the foot of her bed. “I love the barely-there feel of the blanket and use it almost every night as I read stories to my son,” she says. “It provides just enough cover to keep me comfortable without putting me to sleep.” It also features thoughtful, grid-like stitching that gives her whole sleep setup a little extra character.

In true Brooklinen fashion, there are a few distinct hues to choose from, including muted and vibrant colors. “I have this in the storm [colorway], a somewhat dusty blue—and as the name would suggest, it looks like the sky before it rains,” Alpert says. “It almost works like a neutral and pairs well against a crisp white sheet—I use it with a percale cotton set, which has swirls of blue. I also use it with my linen sheets, and it creates this decadent, heirloom-quality bed.”

#5094. 8 Things That Should Always Be in Your Field Bag

Game day is only one part of an athlete’s schedule. These versatile picks from Brooks are made for the rest of your routine.

If you’re a regular at your local soccer league, you probably have your game-day uniform down to a science: cleats, socks, shorts, jersey, done. But you also know that training doesn’t end when you leave the field. There are warmups before practice, conditioning sessions, gym workouts, recovery afterwards, and, of course, the inevitable post-game team hangouts while you’re still wearing half your soccer gear. The right off-field pieces can make all of that a little more comfortable.

Brooks has plenty of options that can slot into those in-between moments—from cushioned running shoes for off-field training to lightweight layers for chilly practices to comfortable sweats for the ride home.

Below are eight of our favorite picks for the training, travel, and downtime surrounding the “beautiful game.”

The Glycerin 23 is arguably Brooks’ most versatile off-pitch training shoe. It’s perfect for road-running and training on hard surfaces thanks to plush cushioning and a smooth, stable ride that’s ideal for conditioning runs, or other training away from the field. The breathable upper and roomy fit also make it a comfortable option when you’re on your feet all day or logging miles without a ball at your feet.

For early-morning practices and chilly fall training sessions, lightweight layers that you can easily move in become essential. After practice, it’s natural to want to keep things comfy. The Reset Hoodie and matching sweatpants make for an easy throw-on-and-go set for cooling down after training, traveling to games, or simply keeping it casual off the field. After kicking off your cleats post-game, the Adrenaline GTS 10 is an ideal recovery shoe. It’s an easy option for walking around after a game or practice, whether you’re heading home, meeting teammates for food, or running a few errands.

Sometimes the best post-practice top is simply one you don’t have to think about. This Graphic Short Sleeve has a relaxed fit and a soft cotton-polyester blend, making it a great choice after training or on a rest day. It’s also versatile enough for gym sessions and other low-key activities when you want something more casual and uncomplicated than your usual performance gear.

For outdoor training days when you need more coverage without piling on heavy layers, the Luxe Long Sleeve offers a truly useful middle ground. Its soft, breathable fabric has odor-resistant properties and UPF 50+ sun protection, while thumbholes help keep the sleeves in place. Wear it on its own during a cool-weather workout or underneath a jacket when temperatures really start to dip.

After spending 90 minutes running around in soccer socks, changing into a fresh pair can feel not only like a luxury, but a necessity. The Reset Crew Socks have a soft cotton blend, plush cushioning, a seamless toe, and arch and midfoot compression for a secure feel. An odor-resistant finish is another welcome feature, whether they’re going straight into your gym bag after practice or carrying you into the rest of your day.

#5093. Justin Sun Accuses Trump-Backed World Liberty of Using UAE Ties to Escape Federal Court

Justin Sun Accuses Trump-Backed World Liberty of Using UAE Ties to Escape Federal Court

Justin Sun’s legal fight with World Liberty Financial now hinges on a question of nationality.

The Tron founder said Tuesday on X that his lawyers have asked a federal court in California to sanction the Trump family-backed crypto venture over how long it took to disclose that its ownership chain gives it citizenship in the United Arab Emirates.

Sun says he is one of World Liberty’s earliest and largest investors. He agreed in November 2024 to buy $30 million of its WLFI tokens and later raised his total investment to $45 million.

In April, he sued the company, alleging it misled him into investing and then unlawfully froze his tokens. World Liberty has fired back in court, alleging that Sun shorted WLFI and drove down its price.

The current dispute turns on a technical point. Sun chose to sue in federal court, which can hear cases between parties from different places but generally can’t hear a foreign plaintiff’s case against a foreign defendant.

As Sun explained it, a company like World Liberty takes on the citizenship of each of its owners, so a single foreign owner makes the whole company foreign for those purposes.

According to Sun, World Liberty’s initial court disclosure listed only itself, with Florida as its sole citizenship. A judge signaled at an August hearing that some of Sun’s claims should stay in open court rather than go to arbitration.

Less than a week later, Sun says, the company amended the disclosure to list 22 members in its ownership chain, with citizenship in Florida, Delaware, Puerto Rico, and the UAE. World Liberty now argues that the federal court lacks jurisdiction over the case, according to Sun.

Sun argues the late disclosure was a tactic to move the case out of federal court, and his motion seeks to make World Liberty and its lawyers bear the cost. He contrasted the company’s position with its own marketing, posting excerpts from World Liberty’s “gold paper” that describe it as “an American, tax-paying, corporation.”

Once the sanctions motion is resolved, Sun said, he will refile his lawsuit in California state court. “I will keep fighting for the transparency this community deserves,” he wrote.

The feud has played out publicly for months. Sun has criticized how World Liberty treats its investors and alleged that the company threatened to burn his WLFI tokens.

#5091. Build Strong Bones in Just Four Weeks With This Plan: Download the PDF Today

Bulletproof your bones with this easy-to-follow workout plan.

With aging comes a number of changes—from shifts in muscle mass and hormones to changes in sleep and bone health. Bone density naturally begins to decline around age 35, but there’s plenty you can do to support strong, healthy bones at every stage of life.

Introducing the WH+ 4 Weeks to Stronger Bones workout plan. This comprehensive program gives you the tools to build strength, support bone density, and keep your body resilient at any age.

Each week includes three strength workouts, two bone-health-focused cardio circuits (one optional), one active recovery day, and one rest day. The typical schedule will look something like this:

Saturday: Bone-Strength Circuit 2 (optional)

Each workout is designed to challenge your major muscle groups and boost your bone health in the process. All workouts are outlined in the PDF, including:

Download the PDF below, and save it to your computer or phone for easy access. Then, use it to follow your workouts, log your numbers, and watch your strength build over time.

The only way to truly build bone strength is to load your bones consistently. Repetition is what helps them become stronger over time, so it’s important for you to find a workout schedule that you know you’ll stick with. Although each workout is assigned to a specific day, feel free to adjust the schedule to fit your life. What matters most is choosing workout days that are realistic for you and following through.

Your body—and bones!—need the right fuel to thrive. Bones are made up of a mix of calcium and other nutrients (in fact, 99 percent of your body’s calcium is stored in your bones!). That makes a nutrient-rich diet a vital part of supporting bone health. Use the nutrition cheat sheet included in this program to help you choose calcium-rich foods and build bone-friendly meals.

Fill in your workout charts as you complete each session. As the weeks go by, pay attention to how the exercises feel, and choose more challenging weights and exercises when you’re ready—and watch your strength, balance, and power all improve. Each gain is a sign your hard work is paying off, and that you’re making meaningful steps toward stronger bones.
